About:

Sui wallets sign whatever a dApp hands them; there's no Blockaid- or ScamSniffer-style firewall at the door. Aegis is that firewall.

Aegis is a real Sui Wallet Standard browser extension. It intercepts every dApp transaction, simulates it on-chain, and runs an AI risk judge (Claude) that explains in plain English what the transaction actually does — flagging drainers, wallet sweeps, untrusted packages, and address poisoning before you sign. A deterministic rule floor hard-blocks catastrophic outflows, so a wrong or jailbroken model can never wave a drain through. Every decision streams to a live dashboard.

For serious holdings, opt-in Vault Mode turns your account into a 2-of-2 multisig whose second signer is an AWS Nitro TEE enclave that independently checks policy and refuses to co-sign drains — enforced on-chain and verified by a Nitro attestation registered on Sui.

### 2. SuiSieve

- Updated: 2026-06-22T01:03:18.023Z

- Logo: https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/projects/133f7933-ab45-4f8e-9cd8-f5120655a9e3/423f0077-72b6-493f-ac93-6b2a1150ba33.png

- youtube: https://github.com/RamonLiao/SuiSieve

Media:
- None listed

About:

[Video in README.md]

- **Business Pain Points**: Web2 creator platforms (such as Patreon or OnlyFans) extract 10% to 50% in platform fees and hold payouts for weeks. Traditional Web3 routing tools are pull-based, requiring recipients to manually claim and pay gas, and lack automated tax reserving or yield generation capabilities.
.

- **Solution**: SuiSieve turns a creator's address into a programmable revenue routing sieve. Utilizing a single PTB, every incoming payment is split automatically according to a predefined config: payouts are pushed directly to collaborators' wallets, tax reserves are segregated, and the remainder is deposited into yield-bearing DeFi positions.
.

- **Key Features**:

- **Atomic Routing**: Splitting, tax reserving, and DeFi yield vault deposits executed in one transaction.

- **Capability-Gated Vaults**: Leverages Sui's native ownership model to lock up tax reserves under cold-wallet `TaxCap` authority.

- **Push-Based Splits**: Collaborators receive funds immediately without manual claim actions or gas costs.

- **Immediate Compounding**: Converts idle creator revenues into interest-bearing assets natively.

- Updated: 2026-06-22T00:58:29.706Z

Media:
- None listed

About:

[Video in README.md]

- **Business Pain Points**: Managing global Web3 payroll is a fragmented process. Wire transfers are slow and expensive, whilst typical crypto payroll tools merely dump stablecoins into a wallet. Employees must manually transfer and deposit earnings into yield protocols, and auditors struggle to reconcile gross-to-net payouts.
.

- **Solution**: Sluice turns a corporate payroll run into a single PTB on Sui. It automates gross-to-net splits atomically, locking tax withholding in an escrow, and routing net earnings into yield-bearing vaults (Scallop/Navi) based on a ratio configured by the employee via their personal `AllocationCap`.
.

- **Key Features**:

- **One-Click Payroll Run**: Atomically executes payroll splits for up to 50 employees in a single transaction block.

- **Employee **`AllocationCap`: Grants workers absolute authority over their personal savings/yield ratios.

- **On-Chain Audit Trails**: Emits signed compliance events including FX snapshots from Pyth.

- **zkLogin Onboarding**: Simplifies hire onboarding with Google auth and sponsored gas fees.

About:

Payfrica is a user-friendly platform designed to help you seamlessly spend digital assets in everyday life. With Payfrica, you can easily convert tokens like USDC and SUI directly into local currencies, ensuring fast, secure, and everyday transactions. Whether you’re paying a bill, sending money home, using our fiat voucher feature to gift a friend or shopping locally, Payfrica makes digital assets accessible, simple, and safe to use.

To make your experience even more effortless, Payfrica now features **S.C.I **(**S**kip** C**ash** **to** I**ntent), our innovative, AI voice assistant. S.C.I enables a completely hands-free experience, allowing you to execute key transactions, book rides, and place everyday orders entirely through simple, conversational voice commands.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/7c474343-3356-4c37-a164-74f0e3c49932/baa26e13-a6e8-4085-a52e-7d35b8dbeb18.png

About:

We don't store who you are — we store who you've been related to.

Dibang turns the world's oldest credit system, the gift economy, into Moi Credit: a privacy-preserving, on-chain score — built to unlock uncollateralized loans on Sui.

Dibang makes your relationships your credit. We turn real-world social ties — the gifts, guestbooks and introductions of everyday life — into Moi Credit, an on-chain, privacy-preserving credit score. We don't store who you are; we store who you've been related to.

Why relationships? Every year, tens of trillions of won (~$25B) move through Korea's 222K weddings and 358K funerals as cash "relationship money" (부조) — and every culture has its own gift economy. It's the richest signal of who you can trust, yet it's all cash: untracked, un-credited, invisible to finance.

Dibang is a live product — a digital wedding invitation, guestbook and cash-gift flow already used at real weddings. Every interaction becomes a relationship signal.

Under the hood — a four-layer pipeline, grounded in category theory and a taxonomy of human relationships:

Layer 1 — Classification (taxonomy). Every relationship event is typed by a taxonomy of event types, action types and role pairs — the raw alphabet of social interaction.

Projection (functor). A projection functor maps each (event × action × role) into a relational signal across the four relational models of human interaction — Communal Sharing, Equality Matching, Authority Ranking, Market Pricing.

Layer 2 — Dynamics (trust ledgers). Signals of the same kind are folded — order-independent, like an abelian group — into relational ledgers: matrices of trust edges (giving / connection) and node-level fulfilment (repayment).

Layer 3 — Credit (Φ). Ledgers are normalized and propagated through the network by matrix power iteration — a reversed-giving PageRank in the EigenTrust lineage. Identity and associativity let these compose as a category; by Yoneda's lemma, a node is determined by its pattern of relationships, not its identity — which is exactly what makes it privacy-preserving.

Layer 4 — Integration. The relational categories are unified into a single trust network and resolved into Moi Credit: one absolute, on-chain score — your position in a living trust network, not the size of any balance.

On Sui, relationships are shared objects and Moi Credit is a composable Move object that can be read anonymously — without ever learning who you are. Deployed on Sui testnet — package · Moi Credit object · relationship shared object: [https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/object/0xf33fba09dcade57bb0a27bd0f0bbd698a18d358c74ae7273d0a85bcab9b7e77d/tx-blocks](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/object/0xf33fba09dcade57bb0a27bd0f0bbd698a18d358c74ae7273d0a85bcab9b7e77d/tx-blocks), with on-chain transactions across 100 wallets.

Where we're headed. Moi Credit is built to become the collateral layer for the gift economy — letting lending protocols read your relationship credit to underwrite uncollateralized, relationship-backed loans on Sui, settled through on-chain DeFi rails like DeepBook. That's our roadmap; it is not live yet.

We don't store who you are — we store who you've been related to. Dibang's vision is to record a hundred years of relationships across all seven billion people, and quantify humanity's entire trust network. Relationships are everything.

About:

**Aperture — a confidential compliance layer for Sui.**

Sui's confidential transfers encrypt amounts on-chain — great for privacy, but they blind the compliance tooling that assumed amounts were readable, and the auditor model that ships with the primitive is a single key that decrypts everything a user does. Regulation is moving the other way: the EU Travel Rule is live, and AMLR bars unidentified institutional accounts in 2027. The answer isn't anonymity — it's **selective disclosure**.

**Built and running on devnet — Mode B (Proof-of-Figure):**

- A holder selects encrypted entries; Aperture aggregates them homomorphically (`Ciphertext.add`) into a **128-byte ElGamalNizk proof**, generated in-browser in **under a second** — proving an aggregate equals a disclosed figure without revealing which entries contributed, their balance, or their key.

- A verifier checks it **off-chain in under 10 ms**, and the same proof verifies **on live devnet** via a read-only `devInspect` to our Move module (`verifier::verify_aggregate`).

- An on-chain test suite confirms valid proofs are accepted and **tampered proofs abort**.

One figure proves a bounded range, so disclosure stays scoped by design — **private from the public, provable to whoever you choose** — aligned with where the Travel Rule, AMLR, and compliance firms like TRM Labs point.

**Honest scope:** this build ships Mode B end to end. Mode A (an auditor console for issuers) is designed, not yet built. Demo keypairs are fixtures, not production HKDF.

Built on Sui's confidential transfers. Team behind PactDa (3rd place, Sui Overflow 2025).

**Live:** [aperture-ivory.vercel.app](http://aperture-ivory.vercel.app) · **Code:** [https://github.com/BonChain/aperture](https://github.com/BonChain/aperture)

- **Business Pain Points**: Web3 onboarding has a steep learning curve. Requiring novices to write down seed phrases, set up wallets, and acquire SUI tokens for gas kills conversion. Additionally, traditional payments are static, missing opportunities to convert transaction events into long-term customer relationships.
.

- **Solution**: KoiQuest turns the social ritual of red packet gifting into a programmable onboarding funnel on Sui. Leveraging zkLogin and sponsored transactions, users claim stablecoins instantly without setting up a wallet or paying gas, immediately unlocking interactive on-chain quests designed to build customer engagement.
.

- **Key Features**:

- **Walletless zkLogin**: Onboards users using Google/Apple accounts in under 60 seconds.

- **100% Sponsored Transactions**: Zero gas fees for senders and recipients.

- **PTB Quest Engine**: Claims, quest generation, and merchant reward locking are chained atomically in one transaction.

- **Walrus Media Integration**: Stores custom red packet skins and merchant assets permanently on Walrus.

About:

Slyce is a deferred collaboration protocol built on Sui. It solves one of the most persistent problems in the creative industry: talented upcoming creators cannot access the professionals they need because those professionals require upfront payment, and the creator does not have that money yet.

Slyce replaces upfront payment with something better. Before any work begins, every collaborator locks in their agreed cut of future earnings. The agreement is immutable. The payout is automatic and atomic the moment earnings arrive. Nobody needs to trust anyone because the protocol enforces the agreement.

An upcoming artist can now walk up to a producer, a marketer, or an established feature and make a credible offer without a single coin in their pocket. Work with me, and you will get paid when this earns. Built on Sui using Programmable Transaction Blocks for atomic multi-party payouts and zkLogin for frictionless onboarding, Slyce is access infrastructure for creators who currently have no path in.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/27c37c9e-f7a0-4059-8402-95686ed7e51d/e70d215b-0a1e-4a5f-b3be-4eac7e1d3520.png

About:

**ConduitCredit issues on-chain USDC credit lines underwritten by TEE-verified income — income, not collateral.**

THE PERSON & THE WALL

Maria drives rideshare and picks up freelance work — about $4,200/month of provable, recurring income, but no idle crypto. Every Sui money market (Suilend, NAVI, Scallop) demands 130–200% over-collateralization, so they only serve people who already hold capital. They are structurally incapable of serving the gig/1099 workers, stablecoin-salary earners, and SMEs whose creditworthiness lives in their income stream — not their balance sheet.

HOW IT WORKS

An AWS Nitro enclave reads Maria's recent income via Plaid, computes the average inside hardware no one can tamper with, and signs an attestation — the raw bank statements never touch the chain. Sui verifies that attestation natively and opens a USDC credit line sized to the income (30% LTV → a $1,260 limit for Maria). Lenders fund a standalone pool split into a junior (first-loss, higher-yield) and senior (protected) tranche; repaid interest lifts tranche share prices, and defaults hit junior first.

WHY IT NEEDS SUI (THE MOAT)

On EVM, trusting an off-chain income number means a third-party oracle that re-signs the data — reintroducing the exact trust you set out to remove. Sui's native sui::nitro_attestation verifies a real AWS Nitro attestation in-contract, with the AWS root CA shipping inside the framework; the per-request check is a cheap on-chain Ed25519 verify_signature. The whole security model is one line: verify_signature accepts a good enclave signature and rejects a bad one. There is no EVM-native equivalent.

LIVE & PROVEN ON TESTNET

• Full loop on testnet: attest → open line → borrow → repay → close — every tx succeeds, and a tampered attestation is rejected on-chain (E_BAD_SIGNATURE).

• register_via_nitro verified against a REAL AWS Nitro attestation (COSE + X.509 to the framework's AWS root CA, PCR-pinned) in the test suite.

• BCS wire format pinned byte-for-byte across Move ↔ Rust (enclave) ↔ JS; 15/15 Move tests including a multi-cycle pool-solvency invariant.

• The live dApp reads income from Plaid (Sandbox) end-to-end.

MAINNET-READY

The pool is generic over the coin type (dUSDC → native USDC, zero contract changes). Two switches flip it live: Plaid Sandbox → production, and the operator-fallback signer → a live Marlin Oyster Nitro enclave via the already-implemented register_via_nitro.

Market validated off-Sui by 3Jane (USD3/sUSD3 tranches) and Huma's receivables vault; uncontested on Sui.

- Created: 2026-06-22T00:07:48.988Z
- Updated: 2026-06-22T00:07:48.988Z

- Logo: https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/projects/74e96bcf-b5e1-4211-af2e-b2160acf44d2/f110145a-88ce-4644-ac95-c4626245fc90.png

Links:

Media:
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/74e96bcf-b5e1-4211-af2e-b2160acf44d2/4fe3f25d-1806-470d-8620-58daac3d1aea.png

About:

The only merchant-initiated, Pyth-USD-capped pull-billing primitive on Sui, with a dead-man-switch will, composed into a single atomic pay-swap-deliver programmable transaction.

**BitcoinVault** ships two live Move primitives on Sui testnet: `btc_mandate` (Pyth-attested pull-billing mandates with USD caps and two-party merchant authorization) and `btc_will` (heartbeat-gated dead-man-switch inheritance). Composed PTBs combine oracle read, capped pull, DeepBook swap, and deliver in one signature.

**Why it is built**

Recurring billing on Sui has no cap-enforced, merchant-initiated pull primitive.
Programmable inheritance (dead-man switch) does not exist as a Move-native object model. Payment rails move tokens; they do not ship authorization objects with oracle-gated caps composable in one PTB.

- 60-second judge demo (3 steps)

1. Open https://btcvault-sui.vercel.app/?preview=1, the dashboard renders without a wallet. Scroll to Pull History: real on-chain `btc_mandate::PullExecuted` events, each carrying the live Pyth-attested SUI/USD price and USD value at pull time, plus a row showing an over-cap pull that aborted on-chain (`EEXCEEDS_USD_CAP`, code 10).

-
2. Open the Composed Pull (one PTB) panel. This is the headline: a single atomic transaction does live Pyth read, USD-cap check, `pull_coin`, DeepBook swap, and delivery. Click Preview PTB to see the 5-command transaction as JSON before signing.

-
3. Verify the proofs on SuiScan using the digests below. The over-cap abort and the two-party pull (payer != merchant) prove the object-capability authorization and the oracle gate are real, not single-wallet theater.

**Standout features**

1. Pyth-gated composed pull PTB (one signature, four effects)

"BitcoinVault is the only DeFi entrant shipping a BTC treasury mandate story. The composed pull panel builds one PTB: live Pyth SUI/USD read, USD-capped `pull_coin`, optional DeepBook swap, deliver DEEP to merchant. All atomic. If any step fails, nothing moves."

See: Composed Pull panel badge oracle + USD-cap + pull + swap → Preview PTB JSON command list. Footer tx [`4UJcvo8X`](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/tx/4UJcvo8XhLT1SJA1hNPJe3nyST8rXkzsZGh6ZE2dDGFo): Pyth update, price assert, pull, swap, deliver.

Kill shot: Count PTB commands on Suiscan. Merchant in deliver command differs from mandate owner on two-party demo.

2. DeepBook depth/slippage gate (abort before funds move)

"Thin pool protection is not a frontend warning. `assert_clears_with_depth_vec` mirrors Splash liquidity math. If the book cannot clear the pull size at max slippage, Move aborts code 12 before any Balance moves."

See: Composed Pull checkbox DeepBook depth/slippage gate (checked by default). Reference abort tx [`DyQ62kyu`](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/tx/DyQ62kyuvyZP2N34b7wQoX4N1fX1dskTZKvJXL1bD3YF) code 12 EINSUFFICIENT_DEPTH.

Kill shot: FAILED effect, gas only. No pull event, no balance delta.

3. Daily aggregate cap (Move abort code 16)

"Per-tap USD caps are table stakes. Daily aggregate cap is the Rails-style defense: three legal pulls in the same UTC day accrue spend, the fourth aborts code 16 even when each tap alone is under the per-tap limit. No single-transaction bypass."

See: Pull History row aborted · code 16 → tx [`DA6kXHWF`](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/tx/DA6kXHWFTWH7RwXdGFD6YHdN1sssb14VuPoxZRKD7f85). UI abort decoder labels Daily USD cap exceeded.

Kill shot: Prior pulls in same day succeeded; cumulative USD crossed daily threshold. Day rolls at `now_ms / 86_400_000`.

4. Two-party merchant authorization (not self-dealing)

"Pull-billing mandates name a merchant address. Only that merchant can call `pull_coin`. Payer A pulling from their own mandate aborts code 4 EUNAUTHORIZED_MERCHANT. Real merchant B pull is on-chain proof this is authorization, not a self-transfer."

See: Pull History row two-party · B 0x685b... → tx [`48MNeX3J`](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/tx/48MNeX3JCWTQgsuaEVca7p9FP8JYLFwfj8nexxH8fhPE). Object lookup mandate `0x965d685e...` Sees merchant != owner.

Kill shot: Contrast with unauthorized payer abort [`HziiHCNS`](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/tx/HziiHCNSeZsNsBawHmfNm2yz9v75keLc2rw7dM2Ct5e1) code 4.

5. `btc_will` heir claim (dead-man switch inheritance)

"Treasury policy is not just billing. `btc_will` is a Move-native dead-man switch: owner heartbeats reset grace; heir claims the full balance after silence. Inclusive >= boundary at grace expiry, tested in Move."

See: Object lookup will `0x67899250...` → rows Heir, Grace period, Last heartbeat, Claimed: No. Will package [`0x9e427faf...`](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/object/0x9e427faf7ca8a8d8c6437005cdd7f0d6b978d95ea7d9a36e7dfb5e522dfddddb).

Kill shot: Heir Inbox Heir Claim appears only after grace elapses without heartbeat.

6. Mandate top-up and revoke (owner lifecycle)

"Mandates are living objects. Owner tops up collateral or revokes authorization from the same card. Revoke is permanent; freeze kill-switch is reversible. Merchant pulls stop immediately on revoke."

See: Your Mandates card → Top-up and Revoke buttons after wallet create. Create form Sees dual caps: Per-tap USD cap and Daily USD cap.

Kill shot: Revoked mandate Sees Revoked: Yes in object lookup; subsequent pull aborts.

About:

**OpenRails **turns a signed payment intent into a bounded, on-chain streaming channel on Sui. It brings the simplicity of a payment link to programmable money: instead of a static "pay me" URL, every link is a live financial channel that streams value by the second and settles with a cryptographically auditable receipt.

**The problem**

Money on-chain still moves in discrete, all-or-nothing transfers. But the machine economy, AI agents buying compute, APIs billing per call, creators selling timed access. needs value to flow continuously, settle concurrently, and prove exactly what was paid. Traditional escrow is slow, custodial, and serializes through global consensus, so swarms of agents can't transact in parallel without contention.

**The approach**

**OpenRails **compiles a signed intent into a **Paycard**, an isolated, single-writer owned object on Sui. Because each channel is its own owned object, it bypasses global consensus sequencing: thousands of agents and merchants can open, stream, and settle channels in parallel at sub-second latency. Value accrues by lazy evaluation (rate × elapsed, capped at the funded pool); no gas burns while value streams; gas is paid only when a channel is opened, claimed, or closed.

**Two primitives, one mental model**

- **RailsCard **(outbound grant): the payer funds a bounded channel now and shares a link. The recipient opens it and claims what has streamed over time. Think: a self-depleting gift card or an agent allowance.

- **RailsFlow **(inbound invoice): the recipient signs terms and shares a link with no on-chain object yet. The payer opens it and funds the channel. Think: a streaming invoice.

Both are produced as a shareable link with a scannable QR code, and the app includes deep-link consumer views

**Settlement you can prove**

Every channel is capital-bounded; it can never pay beyond its pool. When it closes, STN-Delta settlement routes earned value to the recipient and unearned residual back to the payer atomically, emitting a 'SettlementReceipt' event with a hard invariant: paid + residual = allocation. The console renders these as human-readable receipt cards (plain-language outcome, payer → recipient flow, conservation check), and a public receipt API indexes them for any Web2 backend to consume over plain HTTPS.

**Onboarding without friction**

Sign in with Google (zkLogin); no extension, no seed phrase; the wallet is derived from the Google identity. With Enoki gas sponsorship enabled, signed-in users transact gas-free (sponsorship is wired and optional). Standard Sui wallets (Slush/Suiet) are also supported, with a one-click switch to Sui testnet.

About:

**Sweem streams salaries by the millisecond and puts the idle payroll float to work, earning yield for the company while it waits to be claimed.**

Sweem lets organizations stream salaries to employees at per-millisecond granularity, while idle payroll float is automatically invested into yield protocols to generate revenue. Employees claim their earnings continuously and route them into personal vaults with customizable yield strategies. Live on Sui mainnet.

**The Problem**

Traditional payroll is bursty and capital-inefficient. Money sits idle in treasury accounts between pay cycles, earning nothing. Employees wait two weeks to a month to access wages they have already earned. Sweem fixes both: salaries accrue every millisecond, and the unclaimed float earns yield instead of sitting dead.

**What It Does**

- **Per-millisecond payroll streaming**: Organizations fund a stream pool once; employee balances accrue continuously. No pay-cycle batching.

- **Idle-fund yield investing:** Unclaimed payroll is automatically deployed into Navi, Scallop, or Suilend (lending) or Ondo USDY (yield-bearing stablecoin).

- **Atomic claim-with-liquidity:** Employees claim across split yield positions in a single transaction, even when funds are mid-investment.

- **Employee yield vaults: **Personal multi-token vaults with custom allocation across lending protocols, yield stablecoins, or liquid staking.

- **Stream controls:** Org can pause (employee still earns), resume, or permanently stop individual streams.

- **CSV bulk import:** Upload an employee roster; AI plus heuristics auto-detect salary, wallet, and email columns.

- **Live APY discovery:** Frontend queries real-time yields across all integrated protocols.

- **AI payroll assistant:** Natural-language interface (Llama 3.3) to query payroll, manage employees, and control streams. Tool outputs are reviewable, signable transactions.

- **Fee model:** 0.25% deposit fee; yield split (org 10%, treasury 10%) aligns incentives toward reinvestment.

**Architecture**

Sweem cleanly separates on-chain financial state (Move contracts) from metadata and UX (backend plus frontends). Employee streams are discoverable directly from chain even if the backend is down. It chain-first by design.

**Smart Contracts (Move 2024, Sui mainnet)**

- **sweem_core:** StreamPool streaming logic plus EmployeeVault multi-token vaults. Pool creation, deposit/topup, claim, pause/resume/stop, live claimable-amount computation (computed, not stored), excess withdrawal.

- **sweem_registry:** Protocol allowlist, fee config, and role-based access control (FeeManager and ProtocolManager roles).

- **sweem_adapters:** Yield integrations: Navi, Scallop, Suilend, USDY. Hot-potato receipt pattern; USDY does a 2-step extract-then-deposit with Cetus swap routing.

- **sweem_adapters_stsui:** stSUI liquid staking (vault-only).

**Backend (sweem-server)**

Cloudflare Workers (edge) plus Hono. PostgreSQL (Neon) via Hyperdrive, Drizzle ORM. Wallet PersonalMessage signature auth (60s TTL, replay-protected). Cloudflare Workers AI (Llama 3.3-70B) powers CSV mapping and the payroll assistant. Resend for transactional email.

**Frontends**

- **fe**: Org dashboard and treasury panel (Next.js 16, React 19, shadcn/ui).

- **employee**: Employee portal: stream discovery, live balance ticker, claim, vault management.

- **sweem-sdk**: Drop-in React checkout component for USDC/SUI payments.

- **client-test**: Mainnet reference implementation.

Wallet via @mysten/dapp-kit, data via TanStack Query, swaps via Cetus aggregator, charts via Recharts.

**Integrations**

Lending Protocols

- Navi

- Scallop

- Suilend

Yield Bearing Stablecoin

- Ondo USDY

LST

- stSUI: Liquid staking. Employee vault SUI staking.

DEX

- Cetus: DEX aggregator. Swap routing for claim and allocate transactions.

**Tech Stack**

- Contracts: Move 2024, Sui, OpenZeppelin Sui math and access-control.

- Backend: Cloudflare Workers, Hono, Drizzle, Neon Postgres, Workers AI (Llama 3.3), Resend.

- Frontend: Next.js 16, React 19, TypeScript, Tailwind, Framer Motion, dapp-kit, TanStack Query, Cetus SDK.

About:

### **An on-chain proprietary trading firm for crypto traders.** Think FTMO, but verifiable, enforced by code, and owned by the trader on the Sui stack.

It gives you a track record that does not vanish into a firm's private database, bend when a payout is due, or hang on a reviewer's mood. Instead of trusting the firm, you prove your skill against live market prices under rules a smart contract enforces. The proof is yours: portable, tamper-proof, and verifiable by anyone.

**Why it wins:** it's not a toy. It takes a proven, multi-billion-dollar business with a real and widely-felt trust problem, and uses the exact things Sui is good at; objects, capabilities, sponsored transactions, resource safety — to fix that problem at the protocol layer.

**WHAT MAKES ULTRAPROP SPECIAL:**

- **Rules enforced by code.** Profit target, max drawdown, and daily-loss limits live inside the evaluation contract. Break one and the evaluation ends instantly, with no back-office override and no appeal.

- **Verifiable track record.** Every account, trade, and pass or fail is recorded on-chain, so your performance is something you can prove to anyone, not a screenshot you ask people to believe.

- **Proof-of-skill credential.** Passing mints a soulbound Genesis credential, and an on-chain reputation score grows trade by trade. It is a reputation you carry between firms and protocols, not one trapped inside a single app.

- **Auditable capital.** The firm's funding sits in an on-chain vault with on-chain solvency, so capital adequacy is a fact you can check, not a claim you take on faith.

- **Stablecoin payouts.** Profits settle in USDC in minutes, anywhere, with the evaluation fee refunded on your first payout.

- **Invisible chain.** Sign in with email and trade. No wallet, no seed phrase, no gas. The chain is plumbing, not a hurdle.

- **Real markets, modeled honestly.** You trade live crypto-perpetual prices with a deterministic fill-and-slippage model that shows you the exact fill (price, house tilt, fees) before you commit.

**WHY THE SUI STACK MATTERS:**

- **Sui** gives Ultraprop fast finality and an object model where each account, trade, and credential is first-class on-chain state. It is cheap enough to record every trade, not just a sample.

- **Move** gives resource-safe custody for the treasury and vault, so "capital cannot be spent twice" is a language guarantee, not a hope.

- **Sui's capability model** lets operator authority (`AdminCap` and `ExecutorCap`) stay least-privilege and revocable on-chain, so a compromised key is cut off instantly.

- **Sponsored transactions** make onboarding invisible. The firm pays for execution, so traders never touch gas or wallets.

- **A live price and risk pipeline** (a Rust risk engine on a native Hyperliquid mark feed) drives deterministic, one-to-one paper fills against real market conditions.

**In simple terms:**

- Sui is the execution and ownership layer

- Move is the rulebook and the resource-safe treasury

- The on-chain vault is the capital and payments layer

- The Genesis credential is the portable reputation layer

- Ultraprop turns all of that into a usable, trust-minimized prop firm

**SO THE SHORT VERSION IS:**

- Ultraprop is an on-chain prop firm built on the Sui stack

- it enforces the rules in code

- records your track record on-chain

- pays out in stablecoins

- and lets traders prove skill and earn real capital without trusting a black box

**VERIFY IT ON SUI TESTNET:**

Everything below is deployed and live. Open any object in a Sui testnet explorer to see the registries, tier economics, and trade and pass events for yourself.

Package: `0xf5c878892e943217b1104e584a15060212a1468b0dc41b685bd507bf85ccfbfe`

Account Registry: `0x300ed469593be651c2ec0c9155184f03f2d4aa0920b4c5382f075a267de5237b`

Treasury: `0x3d395ecad657e0621a539ad1d3a672de92df9387dbf86b97cf67d2f648853208`

Tier Config: `0x7cc46cf18b73f789db18d944a8e3bf36bdde6e0b4d5c7fd856e381eb14c4f577`

Mock USDC: `0xf97bea36c6fd7558f7fbfa5cd0f5bdf7e4751d33d1ac678ab8161aa32934b12b::usdc::USDC`

**TECH STACK**

*Sui Move, OpenZeppelin Move libraries, @mysten/sui (TypeScript SDK), Privy (email auth + embedded wallets), Pyth (Hermes price feed), Hyperliquid (live perp market data), Rust (risk engine: Tokio, Axum, SQLx), WebAssembly (deterministic sim core), Hono (API gateway), PostgreSQL, Next.js, React, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS, Turborepo, Biome, Vercel.*

---

*Live today on Sui testnet as a paper-trading closed beta. Real prices, real contracts, no real capital at stake yet. The funded firm is the next phase: prove it, never just promise it.*

About:

### **FlowGate — Hackathon Project Description**

###
**Inspiration:
**The disagreement between Sam Altman and Elon Musk over OpenAI scraping X wasn't really about two billionaires. It exposed a structural problem with the AI economy: there is no programmatic way for a website or service to say "you can access my data, but you pay per second." AI agents are consuming the internet — research, market data, social signals, legal databases — but the underlying payment infrastructure was built for humans. Credit cards require KYC. Monthly subscriptions aren't granular enough. API keys expose your business logic. And none of these work for an autonomous agent operating at machine speed without a human in the loop. We kept coming back to the same question: what if an agent could just pay for what it needs, on the fly, the moment it needs it, and stop paying the moment it's done? That's what FlowGate is.

**What it does:**

FlowGate is a **money streaming SDK for AI agents on Sui**. It gives autonomous agents their own wallets and lets them stream SUI micropayments per second of service access — without human approval, invoices, or billing cycles.

The core mechanic is simple:

- An AI agent tries to access a service

- It receives an HTTP **402 Payment Required** response with streaming payment terms

- The FlowGate SDK intercepts this, **automatically creates a StreamObject on Sui** using the agent's own wallet

- The service middleware verifies the stream balance on-chain and grants access

- The moment the stream stops — access is revoked **instantly**

No trust required on either side. The payment and the access are the same object on-chain.

**The SDK is platform-agnostic.** Any service that wants to charge AI agents per second of access can integrate FlowGate's x402 middleware in a few lines. The demo makes this concrete with a **website scraping marketplace**: site owners list their URLs with a price per second, agents discover listings, open streams, and scrape. The demo is one instantiation — the SDK is the product.

Key technical facts:

- Each agent gets its own **Ed25519 keypair** generated on creation — agents spend from their own wallets, not the user's

- Payment streams are **first-class Sui objects** — `StreamObject<T>` is a generic Move struct that works with any coin type

- Access control happens **on-chain** via `seal_approve_stream` Move policy — there's no off-chain session, cookie, or trust

- One command starts everything: `docker compose up --build`

### **How we built it:**

**Sui Move contract** — A generic `StreamObject<phantom T>` that holds the deposited coin, tracks rate per second, and uses `sui::clock::Clock` for time-based balance calculation. Deployed to Sui Testnet: `0xb05b3964df8b88a86cda6b192893399966014af9dd6fc6beb26f1343a0495495`

**FlowGate SDK (TypeScript)** — Wraps `@mysten/sui` v2.x to handle the full agent payment lifecycle: intercept 402 → build PTB → create StreamObject → retry request with stream ID header → close stream and reclaim unspent funds. Uses `SuiJsonRpcClient` from `@mysten/sui/jsonRpc` with confirmed-working v2.x import patterns.

**x402 Gateway (Express + Node.js)** — Middleware that reads `X-StreamEngine-Stream-Id` from request headers, calls `sui_getObject` via RPC, extracts the stream balance from the Move object fields, and gates or denies access. Providers register their endpoints with a rate per second; agents discover them via `GET /api/providers`.

**Agent wallet management** — Server-side Ed25519 keypair generation per agent. Private keys are encrypted at rest with AES-256-CBC using a required `AGENT_KEY_SECRET` environment variable. Private keys never appear in API responses.

**Persistence + deployment** — SQLite via `better-sqlite3` for agent and provider records that survive server restarts. Docker Compose runs the Node server and nginx-served React client as a two-container stack, with a named volume for the SQLite database. One command: `docker compose up --build`.

**Frontend** — React + Vite dashboard with two views: Provider Hub (list a website with price per second, watch live earnings) and Agent Terminal (browse listed sites, open streams, watch live balance, close and refund).

**What we learned:**

Sui's object model is genuinely different from account-based chains, and it matters for this use case. A `StreamObject` isn't just a balance — it's an on-chain object that carries the payment terms, the recipient, the rate, and the deposited funds all together. Access control can be tied directly to object existence and balance. This is cleaner than anything you could build on Ethereum without a lot of extra infrastructure.

Programmable Transaction Blocks are powerful but unforgiving. The ability to bundle coin splitting, object creation, and Move calls into a single atomic transaction is exactly what agent payment flows need. But getting the argument types right — especially the difference between `tx.pure.address()`, `tx.pure.u64()`, and `tx.object()` — requires reading the actual Move contract, not guessing.

Multi-model AI coding across long sessions is a real coordination problem. Different models have different failure modes: some overclaim success without running tests, some introduce hardcoded secrets as convenience fallbacks, some get lost when the project context grows too large. The solution is maintaining an `AGENTS.md` file with verified technical facts, confirmed working code patterns, and explicit lists of what to trust vs verify.

**What's next for FlowGate:**

**Agent-to-agent streaming.** The current flow is agent-to-service. The same SDK supports agent-to-agent payments — one AI service paying another for a capability, with no human intermediary. This is the foundation of an autonomous AI economy.

**Privacy layer via Sui Confidential Transfers.** All streams are currently public on-chain — amounts and participants visible. Sui Confidential Transfers (currently in devnet beta as of June 2026) will hide stream amounts while preserving validity proofs. When this ships to mainnet, FlowGate streams inherit hidden amounts with no contract changes required.

**USDC and multi-token streams.** The `StreamObject<phantom T>` contract is already generic. Supporting Circle USDC on Sui testnet is a type argument change — `StreamObject<USDC>` instead of `StreamObject<SUI>`. Stablecoin streams make pricing human-readable and remove volatility from agent budgets.

**On-chain provider registry.** The current provider registry is off-chain (SQLite). `contracts/sources/registry.move` is written but not yet deployed. Migrating to an on-chain registry means any agent can discover any provider permissionlessly, without going through FlowGate's server.

**NPM package release.** Publishing `@flowgate/sdk` so any platform can integrate agent payment streaming in three lines of TypeScript.

About:

**Fullmetal is a contract execution venue and collateral management protocol for Institutional OTC derivatives that utilises risk-responsive collateral rehypothecation and cross-margining to build the missing collateral efficiency layer for institutional finance.**

---

---

**The Problem :**

Trillions of dollars in collateral sit completely idle in the OTC (Over-the-Counter) derivatives market.

-

This collateral remains locked behind a stack of intermediaries that each add fees, latency, and coordination overhead.

-

In traditional finance, big-money institutional derivatives trades don't go through exchanges. Instead, large-scale positions are routed through bilateral, bespoke contracts in the OTC market. To trade derivatives, institutions must post collateral. But with no common coordination layer or shared ledger, up to ten layers of regulatory intermediaries are mandated to ensure collateral is managed properly.

-

This causes the following problems:

-

--> Each intermediary takes a fee cut.

-

--> Every intermediary layer compounds administrative complexity.

-

--> Settlement is slow and threshold-gated by minimum transfer amounts, with most institutions still running manual margin-call flows.

-

--> Different intermediary types exist for different derivative classes, but there is no cross-derivative or cross-intermediary margining possible, thus leading to greater collateral requirements.

-

--> Although standardised exchange-traded derivatives face the collateral efficiency problem too, the absence of a central clearing counterparty and contract standardisation makes collateral management and coordination significantly larger pain points in OTC.

-

---

---

**The Solution :**

Fullmetal provides the full-stack execution and collateral efficiency layer - just one protocol that replaces every intermediary in traditional institutional OTC derivatives.

Fullmetal's primary focus is unprecedented capital efficiency achieved through:

-

--> risk-responsive collateral rehypothecation to a multitude of yield-bearing venues.

--> cross-margining across derivative types to reduce initial margin requirements for institutional clients, thus allowing higher risk-adjusted leverage.

---

---

**Competitive Edge and Target Market :**

Several companies attempt to solve for either institutional OTC or collateral efficiency in very different ways, from Variational Pro to BlackRock's BUIDL. However, the solutions either approach the problems in ways that make DeFi integration difficult (essentially TradFi wearing a crypto suit) or fail to design for the real capital efficiency gains that replacing traditional OTC infrastructure should unlock.

-

Fullmetal's edge is a genuinely crypto-native design built around mathematical elegance and maximal use of the utility blockchains actually provide: moving money reliably, instantaneously, and programmatically to **turn collateral itself into a generative primitive.**

-

Fullmetal's target clients are crypto-first trading desks and smaller firms that would realise meaningful savings using Fullmetal over prime brokerage.

---

---

**Demo and Roadmap :**

The demo covers -

-

--> The full onboarding flow for an institutional trading desk.

-

--> Admin and trader dashboards.

-

--> OTC contract creation for both direct and RFQ-mediated trades.

-

--> Collateral rehypothecation to the DeepBook Margin pool.

-

--> A live demonstration of mock oracle price changes atomically triggering risk-responsive collateral withdrawal from DeepBook Margin and redeposit once the volatility indicator normalises.

-

**The demo supports forwards, perpetuals, and forward-perp hybrid contracts.**

Any derivative type can be traded: equities, forex, crypto, and more. All settlements and accepted collateral in initial versions are in USDC. Rehypothecation to DeepBook Margin has been integrated with Suilend and Navi Protocol in progress.

The Near-term roadmap (4 months) is as follows:

-

--> Detailed algorithm for rehypothecation venue risk management.

-

--> Cross-margining.

-

--> Rigorous backtesting across market regimes and edge cases.

-

--> Capital efficiency benchmarking against TradFi equivalents and competing crypto OTC products.

-

--> Real institutional on-ramp integration

-

--> Privacy layer for OTC trades.

-

--> Encrypted activity and decision logs (Seal + Walrus) - accessible only to auditors and the parent institution.

-

--> Proprietary AI layer for monitoring rehypothecation venue activity and dynamically evaluating risk.

---

---

About:

**A continuous prediction market that doesn't make you guess in buckets.**

Most prediction markets force you into yes/no boxes. Will ETH hit exactly $5,000 by EOY? Yes or no. But that's not really how belief works — you might think ETH lands *somewhere between* $4k and $6k, with $5k as your best guess. Continuum lets you bet that way: on a continuous curve, at any strike price you want, all inside one shared pool instead of a hundred fragmented ones.

It's built on Sui, written in Move, and priced by actual Gaussian math running fully on-chain.

### Why this exists

Platforms like Polymarket need a brand new pool for every strike price. "Will BTC hit $100k? Yes/No." "Will BTC hit $110k? Yes/No." Multiply that across every price point people care about, and you get liquidity sliced into a hundred thin puddles instead of one deep pool.

Continuum collapses all of that into a single curve shaped like a bell — a normal distribution — where:

- **μ (mu)** is the market's collective best guess

- **σ (sigma)** is how confident or uncertain that guess is

Bet at any strike price along that curve, and the price you pay is derived directly from where your bet sits relative to everyone else's.

### The core idea, in plain terms

- **One pool, infinite strikes.** No more choosing which discrete pool to fund — capital efficiency goes up because liquidity isn't split a thousand ways.

- **Bettors move the curve. LPs don't.** Every trade nudges μ and σ based on stake-weighted conviction. Liquidity providers are just collateral — they can't quietly shift the curve in their favor, which closes off a manipulation loophole that plagues other AMM designs.

- **You're judged against reality, not the crowd's opinion.** μ is just the market's belief — settlement happens against the real, externally observed outcome. Betting heavily to drag the curve around doesn't change who actually wins.

- **The math lives on-chain, for real.** Error functions, exponentials, square roots — all computed in Move using fixed-point arithmetic, not estimated off-chain and trusted blindly.

### How it resolves

Continuum doesn't lean on one fragile resolution method — it has three:

- **Pyth oracle (price markets)** — fully trustless, permissionless, anyone can trigger it once the market closes and the feed is fresh.

- **Multi-agent AI oracle (everything else)** — an ensemble of LLMs independently estimates the answer for things like sports or news outcomes, and only auto-resolves when they agree with high confidence. If they don't agree, a human steps in.

- **Manual timelock (the fallback)** — owner-proposed, with a 24-hour window for anyone to scrutinize before it locks in.

### A few other things worth knowing

- **Kiosk-Positions are tradeable.** Since every bet is its own on-chain object (not just a balance), you can sell your belief before the market even resolves, via a built-in secondary marketplace.

- **Fees pay LPs automatically.** A MasterChef-style accumulator distributes the 1% trading fee to liquidity providers without expensive on-chain loops.

- **No proxies, no clones, no factory contract gymnastics.** Sui's object model means each market is just one shared object — simpler, cheaper, and easier to reason about than the usual EVM contract sprawl.

### The stack

Move smart contracts on Sui Testnet, an Express + [Socket.io](http://Socket.io) backend streaming live curve updates, and a React + d3 frontend that renders the whole thing like a quant trading terminal rather than a typical DeFi dashboard.

About:

ackstop — an on-chain insurance market and programmable protected payments on Sui.

Sui has no native way to transfer risk. When something breaks, the only backstop is human emergency action — a $223M Sui exploit was "resolved" by validators voting to roll back the chain. Less than 2% of DeFi is insured. Backstop is the missing primitive: a fully-collateralized, oracle-settled insurance market, live on Sui mainnet.

It's a two-sided market. On one side, anyone can buy protection against a stablecoin losing its peg (suiUSDe below $0.985) — pick a coverage amount and term, and the premium is priced live by pool utilization. On the other side, anyone can underwrite — deposit SUI into the cover pool to back policies and earn the premiums. The pool is always fully collateralized (it can never sell more cover than it can pay), with no leverage.

SafePay makes protection programmable. A single atomic transaction (PTB) sends a payment and attaches recipient-owned depeg cover, delivering the payment plus the policy together. Because it's atomic, if the asset is too close to depegging the cover leg aborts and the payment doesn't settle — you can't accidentally pay someone into a breaking asset. This is a genuinely risk-aware payment, proven on mainnet.

Settlement is trustless. Claims settle by reading the Pyth oracle directly — with a confidence band and a sustained-breach dwell window so a single bad tick can't pay, plus an activation delay against last-second adverse selection. There's no claims committee; a guardian can pause new sales but can never block a payout. An autonomous AI underwriter even prices risk and supplies capital on its own, logging every decision verifiably to Walrus.

Not just stablecoins. The contract is asset-agnostic — the same primitive becomes crash/downside cover for any Pyth-priced asset (BTC, ETH, SUI), with the floor set below spot. suiUSDe depeg cover is the first product.

Status — live and verifiable on Sui mainnet: the full lifecycle (buy → arm → confirm → claim) is proven on-chain, SafePay is proven on-chain, the v6 contract is audit-hardened and DEP_ONLY upgrade-locked with admin keys in custody, and npm run verify:public passes 50/50. Try it at [backstop.gudman.xyz](http://backstop.gudman.xyz); every claim is verifiable on the /proof page.

About:

**iSub is a non-custodial recurring + pay-as-you-go payment primitive on Sui — one rail for every subscription *and* for the AI-agent economy.**
A user signs a single, capped, revocable on-chain **mandate**, and from then on the service simply *pulls* within it: a flat monthly creator subscription, a metered water-and-power bill, an AI agent paying per API call — all on the same primitive.
It's **x402-native and AP2-aligned**, so autonomous agents pay through the emerging agent-commerce standards by presenting a *proof-of-possession* — never the user's keys, never a fresh signature per call.
And it's **fully non-custodial**: funds stay in the user's own Account, the contract enforces every limit (per-charge, rate, lifetime budget, expiry), and the merchant can never over-pull or freeze a balance — the user can cancel and withdraw anytime. Merchants ship it in minutes: drop the embeddable checkout (one tiny loader) or call the TypeScript SDK — no custody to hold, no keeper to operate.

About:

BitCred brings credit scoring to DeFi lending on Bitcoin. We analyze a user's Bitcoin on-chain history  - hodl duration, transaction frequency, balance stability - to generate an AI-driven score (650 - 850) registered on Sui. Better scores unlock lower collateral ratios (as low as 110% vs the industry-standard 150%), rewarding trustworthy holders with real borrowing power. Score audit logs are stored on Walrus and referenced on-chain, with all RPC calls routed through Tatum.

About:

Nearby is a self-custodial mobile payments app on Sui, built so paying onchain feels like a normal payments app instead of a crypto wallet. A user signs in with an ordinary OAuth account and Nearby derives a Sui wallet via zkLogin; no seed phrase; the ephemeral key is minted inside the device's Secure Enclave / StrongBox and never leaves. then holds USDC and pays anyone by a human-readable name like `alice@variance` for zero gas. Plain stablecoin transfers ride Sui's native gasless stablecoin primitives, and the few operations that can't be gasless (`variance::custody::register_initial`, agent setup) are sponsored by a 3-of-5 AVS operator quorum that co-signs a Sui multisig  so the user never holds or thinks about SUI, and the backend is never in the payment hot path.

What sets Nearby apart is **autonomous payments**: a user funds a shared on-chain `variance::agents::AgentSpendHub<T>` and authorizes a headless, *unextractable* device key to pay on their behalf, gated in Move by per-transaction and daily limits and able to touch only the pre-funded hub, never the user's main balance. That hub is what lets Apple Intelligence settle a payment by voice, "Hey Siri, pay alice twenty dollars with Nearby" resolves the recipient on-device and calls `agent_transfer<…::usdc::USDC>` on Sui, hands-free, inside the limits the user set onchain. Every send is a real Sui transaction, explorer-verifiable, with hub deposits and agent spends labeled distinctly in an activity feed.

Names are genuine SuiNS leaf subnames minted through an Enoki-style shared-custody object (`variance::custody`) inside the user's *own* sponsored transaction, so `alice@variance` is owned by the user, not by us. Funding works from anywhere: an on-demand Circle programmable-wallet address bridges deposits to the user's Sui address over Mayan MCTP, and [Bridge.xyz](http://Bridge.xyz) virtual bank accounts onramp fiat. Both settling as USDC on Sui, while profile media lives on Walrus. Purpose built for **both iOS and Android from a single Sui SDK we wrote in Swift** and compiled to Android through `swift-java` (jextract).

About:

Swiift is a non-custodial crypto wallet built around Sui's **IKA network dWallets** — a threshold-cryptography (2PC-MPC) system where a wallet's private key is never held in one place. The key is split between the user (a share deterministically derived from a server-held secret + a per-wallet session ID) and the IKA validator network, which jointly produce signatures via DKG/presign/sign protocols. A single dWallet can then control assets across multiple chains (Sui, Solana, Ethereum/Sepolia, and a Bitcoin path via multisig).

About:

**TEK247 — Trustless on-chain laptop repair & resale**

TEK247 turns laptop repair and resale into a trustless on-chain experience, built on top of a **real, operating repair business** in Nigeria — not a concept.

Customers pay into a **milestone escrow in USDC using just a Google login** (zkLogin with sponsored gas — no wallet, no seed phrase, no crypto knowledge). Funds release stage-by-stage only on the customer's approval, with deadline and dispute protections for both sides; a neutral arbiter can act *only* on a disputed deal and can only split the contested funds — never drain them.

Every completed repair is recorded as a **verifiable Device Passport on Sui**. The full diagnostic report and photos are stored on **Walrus** and anchored on-chain by content hash, so the passport travels with the device on resale and any future buyer can verify its true repair history. This directly attacks the counterfeit-spec and stolen-device fraud that plagues emerging-market second-hand electronics.

About:

***"Programmable, private micropayments on Sui — gasless, confidential, composable"***

**The problem?**

Every payment rail shares the same shape: work happens continuously, but settlement is batched into invoices, paychecks, net-30 terms — not because that's better, but because a transfer has historically cost too much to do per-second. A freelancer who finishes work today still waits days for a cross-border wire to clear, the client who can optimize payment costs, looses this posibility.

**What Streamline does?**

A client locks the a project amount in a Move shared object up front. As milestones are approved, funds drip to the freelancer in real time — gasless.

Because the funds arecommited to stream on-chain, the same value is usable while it streams:

- Auto-yield — a configurable slice of every drip routes into a yield vault (8% APR, seeded buffer) and starts compounding the second it's earned (`create_stream_v2` / `drip_with_yield`).

- Borrow against a stream — a `DRIPPING` stream has a present value; a non-transferable `CollateralReceipt` lets the freelancer borrow up to 90% of remaining value from a lending pool (12% APR, seeded liquidity) before the stream finishes paying out.

- Confidential amounts — a per-stream toggle moves balances and drip sizes into Poseidon commitments, with every drip justified by an on-chain Groth16 transfer + range proof (`sui::groth16`, BN254) rather than the Bulletproofs/ristretto255 path, which is devnet-only. The two parties' secret openings are Seal-encrypted and decrypted client-side; the chain never sees a plaintext amount.

**What's actually live?**

This isn't a mocked-up flow: the Move package (state machine, drip math, dispute resolution, yield vault, lending pool, confidential balances) is deployed on Sui testnet with public, verifiable object addresses, backed by 19 tests across state machine, accrual, dispute, yield, lending, and ZK proof logic. A Rust indexer (REST + WebSocket, Postgres-backed) and a permissionless keeper handle settlement and auto-approval. zkLogin via Enoki gives seedless onboarding and sponsors gas, so a user with zero SUI can stream USDC end-to-end. The zk circuits (circom/snarkjs, Poseidon) are validated both in Move tests and against a Rust arkworks serializer to guarantee byte-identical proof formats between browser and on-chain verifier.

**Where it goes?**

The architecture was built to swap testnet stand-ins for production primitives with minimal redesign: mock-USDC → native USDC over Sui's Address Balances for true protocol-level gasless transfers, and the interface-compatible `yield_vault`/`LendingPool` → Scallop's real markets, a 1:1 call-site change rather than a rewrite. The underlying primitive isn't specific to freelance work — milestones generalize to pay periods, vesting cliffs, or subscription renewals, which puts payroll, grants, and SaaS billing on the same rails without a redesign.

About:

Bequest: programmable conditional transfers (on-chain succession) for self-custody, built on Sui.

The Problem:

Self-custody has a fatal gap: if you lose access or pass away, your crypto is gone forever, there's no beneficiary, no recovery, no will.

The Solution:

Bequest is the continuity layer that fixes this without giving up custody while you're alive.

An owner escrows assets into an on-chain Estate and names recipients with basis-point splits. Assets stay fully in the owner's control until a trustless on-chain condition fires, then liquid SUI and assigned key+store objects distribute atomically in a single PTB. The flagship use case is inheritance, but the primitive is broader: any programmable conditional transfer.

What makes it work for real people:

Heirs don't need crypto. A beneficiary claims with Google sign-in (zkLogin) and Enoki-sponsored, gasless transactions, no seed phrase, no owner key, no gas. Browser-verified end-to-end on the live app.

Private last wishes. An encrypted letter is stored on Walrus and decrypts via Seal only after the trigger fires, and only for a bound heir.

Multiple trustless triggers, all proven live on testnet: a dead-man's switch (inactivity timeout), a scheduled release, m-of-n guardian recovery (rotate the owner key at quorum), and a signature-gated attestation trigger (the on-chain half of a Nautilus/TEE verifiable trigger).

Programmable distribution: linear-with-cliff vesting (post-trigger drip), plus a keeper that auto-distributes every escrowed coin and object type so inheritance executes even if no heir claims.

Status:

Live on Sui testnet at [bequest.gudman.xyz](http://bequest.gudman.xyz) public repo, CI (web + keeper + sui move test, 32/32), a no-secret on-chain verifier for judges, and the vesting/guardian/attestation differentiators each proven with real on-chain transactions on the /proof page. Owner setup, gasless heir claim, and executor pause all run from a Google sign-in.

Honest Boundary:

Bequest is a technical succession primitive on testnet, it demonstrates custody, trustless triggering, and atomic distribution, not legal/tax estate enforcement.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/d8693513-6fc9-47e4-b92d-ae2b0e31a38c/03079209-d6c6-4e5b-ab18-2885dde39209.png
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/d8693513-6fc9-47e4-b92d-ae2b0e31a38c/338d3051-212b-4b64-b9f1-5ec5641b907d.jpeg

About:

ENCLAVE is a private intent-settlement protocol that eliminates solver-level MEV from dex routing on the SUI network. Built natively to leverage SUI's object-centric runtime, ENCLAVE seals the entire off-chain solver competition inside a hardware-attested Trusted Execution Environment (TEE), verifies results on-chain via native Move cryptographic primitives, and settles the winning fills atomically against DeepBook v3. By anchoring full auction records to Walrus, it introduces an independently verifiable audit trail that guarantees provably fair execution without relying on a trusted relayer or external verifier contracts.

### **Inspiration**

Intent-based routing architectures promise optimal user pricing through open solver competition, but current implementations suffer from a fundamental design flaw: they leak trade configurations and quotes before settlement. When a solver's bid is exposed to its peers or an untrusted relayer, it triggers three severe economic exploits:

- **Quote Sniping:** Solvers observe a competitor's near-winning route and marginally undercut it, stealing the settlement fee while offering no meaningful improvement to the user.

- **Collusive Floor Setting:** Solver pools form off-chain cartels, keeping quotes artificially close to the user's maximum slippage bound.

- **Settlement-Time Extraction:** Relayers or front-running solvers interleave transactions at the venue level to extract value from the user's fill right at the moment of execution.

On account-based blockchains, solving this requires complex, bolted-on verifier contracts, gas-intensive nonce tracking tables to prevent transaction replays, and a prayer that nothing gets wedged between the "verification" and "payout" steps.

Furthermore, object-based chains introduce a subtler vulnerability: if verification and fund release are split across separate transactions, the intermediate state of a shared object becomes globally readable, creating an extraction window for attackers.

### The Fix

ENCLAVE resolves these vulnerabilities structurally by combining isolated hardware execution with SUI’s unique runtime primitives:

### 1. Sealed TEE Solver Competition

Instead of exposing order flows, all solver quotes are gathered and processed inside a **GCP Confidential Space** enclave. Memory isolation ensures that neither competing solvers nor the enclave operator can peer into the active bid pool. The winning route is determined deterministically inside the hardware using a strict selection criteria:

$$\operatorname{argmax}(\text{Output Amount})$$

The enclave then signs an `Attestation` containing the winning fill parameters.

### 2. Native Move Cryptographic Verification & Linearity

Instead of deploying a separate, complex verifier contract package that must be compiled and maintained, ENCLAVE performs attestation verification natively inside the settlement module itself. It executes a single, highly optimized SUI stdlib call:

`sui::ecdsa_k1::secp256k1_ecrecover`

Replay protection is handled **structurally** by SUI's object model rather than an explicit database or nonce table. User funds are locked inside an owned `Intent<In, Out>` object. Because the SUI runtime enforces object linearity, the `Intent` object is entirely consumed and deleted during settlement, making a replay transaction physically impossible.

### 3. Atomic PTB Composition

Using SUI's Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTBs), ENCLAVE collapses **Verify e** into one single, indivisible transaction. The winning route is executed directly against DeepBook v3's real on-chain order books (`Pool<Base, Quote>`). Because a PTB executes atomically, no adversarial transaction can be interleaved between the moment the TEE signature is verified and the moment the final `Coin<Out>` is delivered to the user.

### 4. Walrus-Anchored Audit Trails

To prevent the TEE from becoming an unverifiable black box, the enclave must stream the entire quote log (every bid submitted, not just the winner's) to **Walrus**. The resulting `walrus_blob_id` is baked directly into the signed on-chain attestation. Anyone can fetch this blob from Walrus to independently recompute the auction math, ensuring the enclave is acting transparently.

### Progress During Hackathon

ENCLAVE was designed and built from scratch during this buildathon, transforming first-principles MEV resistance into functional SUI infrastructure:

- **Core Move Architecture:** We engineered the foundational Move package, cleanly dividing logic across the object-native escrow engine (`intent_pool`), the core verification logic (`settlement`), and the DeepBook v3 liquidity interface (`deepbook_router`).

- **Cryptographic & Mathematical Testing:** We wrote an extensive Move unit-testing suite. This included validating `ecdsa_k1` signature recovery against strict hardware-generated fixtures, enforcing hash-chain continuity across blocks, and testing fixed-point Exponential Moving Average (EMA) math for our Phase 2 solver reputation tracking.

- **Off-Chain Solver Engine:** We built the off-chain solver infrastructure as a high-performance Rust workspace leveraging `tokio` for asynchronous execution, `axum` for solver communication, and `sui-sdk` combined with `fastcrypto` and `bcs` for generating on-chain-compatible payloads.

- **Gas and Storage Optimization:** We successfully integrated SUI's storage-rebate mechanism. By explicitly deleting the `Intent` object upon successful settlement, the protocol reclaims roughly 99% of its initial storage deposit, heavily subsidizing the gas cost of execution.

- **End-to-End Integration:** We spun up a local SUI test validator and automated faucet to run full-lifecycle simulation tests—successfully routing mock intents from submission through TEE auction selection, slippage floor enforcement, and final DeepBook settlement.

About:

### **Veto**

**A deterministic, **verifiable** policy gate for AI agents that hold and move money on Sui.

**Built for:** Sui Overflow 2026 — Agentic Web track **Network:** Sui Testnet **Live demo:**

### **The pitch in one paragraph**

AI agents are starting to hold real wallets (Truth Terminal, ElizaOS, Coinbase Agentic Wallets — see "Evidence" below). Most agent frameworks let the model decide *and* execute in the same step. **Veto puts two enforcement layers between an agent's reasoning and its wallet:** an off-chain deterministic policy engine (runtime, fast, editable) AND an on-chain vault (backstop, hard-capped, tamper-evident). Both must agree for a transaction to land. If the off-chain engine is compromised, the on-chain caps still hold. If a rule is silently edited, the on-chain commit hash diverges from what the feed shows was enforced.

### **The single sentence that matters**

**The off-chain policy engine is the runtime. The on-chain vault is the backstop. Both must agree for a transaction to land.** If the off-chain engine is compromised, the on-chain caps still hold.

About:

Faven helps crypto holders get paid to wait for prices they want.

Cash Secured Puts and Covered Calls are two most used option trading strategies among retail market participants. Faven connects crypto holders with Market Makers and Automated Vault Strategies and turns them into programmable capital management. It reframes complex options logic into a consumer-friendly product: limit orders that pay you.

Set a price. Get paid. Love either outcome.

About:

The world's most-used savings instrument, made trustless: an on-chain ROSCA protocol on Sui with built-in yield, a native DEX swap, and real fiat on/off-ramps for the 600M+ people who already save in rotating circles.

Over 600 million people worldwide save money the same way: in rotating savings circles. Susu in West Africa. Arisan in Indonesia. Tanda, chit funds, hui. Every cycle, every member pays in, and one member takes the pot, moving an estimated $1T+ a year, almost entirely on trust, online groups, and paper ledgers. When an organizer defaults or absconds with the pot, there's no recourse. We surveyed over 10,000 active ROSCA participants before writing a line of code: 92% are dissatisfied with their current circle, and 84% said they'd switch immediately to a trustless alternative.

Susu Protocol rebuilds the ROSCA as a Move smart contract on Sui, and treats it as a full payments product, not just an escrow contract, across three pillars:

- Trustless Core: Move shared objects encode the entire circle lifecycle (create, join, contribute, disburse) so no organizer can tamper with the pot. Contribution and disbursement flows are atomic Programmable Transaction Blocks, so partial failure ( funds stranded mid-flow ) is structurally impossible. Payout order can be fixed or randomized via commit-reveal, and on-chain dispute resolution (raise/vote/resolve) replaces platform arbitration.

- DeFi Engine: idle pool funds between cycles route into a yield vault (Scallop-shaped, ~3% APY) instead of sitting dormant. A member holding only SUI can fund a USDC-denominated contribution through a live DeepBook v3 swap, at the order-book price, with no centralized exchange, in the same flow, with inline shortfall recovery if their balance comes up short.

- Fiat Rails: this is the differentiator most ROSCA-on-chain projects skip. Transak onramp and offramp are wired in and working today, 18 currencies, 160+ countries, bank transfer, M-Pesa, UPI, PIX, GCash, card. A trader in Lagos funds her circle in Naira and cashes out her payout in Naira; the protocol enforces everything in USDC underneath.

Onboarding uses Enoki zkLogin: sign in with Google, get a Sui address. No wallet, no seed phrase, no browser extension, the only onboarding path that works for users who've never touched crypto. Every on-time contribution mints a ContributionBadge, and a MembershipToken accumulates a public, portable reputation score across circles, a credit history for people who've never had one, owned by the member and verifiable on-chain forever.

Susu Protocol is live on Sui Testnet today, with the full circle lifecycle, fiat rails, DeepBook swap, and reputation system shipped and working end to end.

Live app: [https://susuprotocol.vercel.app/](https://susuprotocol.vercel.app/)

Repo: [https://github.com/Susu-Protocol/susu](https://github.com/Susu-Protocol/susu)

TECH STACK

Sui Move (smart contracts) · Programmable Transaction Blocks · Enoki zkLogin · DeepBook v3 · Scallop-shaped yield vault · Transak (fiat on/off-ramp) · Next.js frontend

WHAT'S NEXT

Mainnet deployment with live Scallop pool wiring, USSD/feature-phone access for users without smartphones, IOS/Android mobile app with social communities, automated offramp settlement, and a credit-scoring API exposing on-chain reputation to third-party lenders.

### **User validation**

we ran a digital and in-person review with people currently participating in informal rotating savings circles across Africa, South Asia, and Latin America, sourced from Telegram, WhatsApp, and Facebook ROSCA groups, plus offline interviews with informal traders who run circles day to day.

**10,000+ **participants surveyed, all currently active in an informal rotating savings circle, **92% **are dissatisfied with the circle they currently participate in, **84% **said they would join Susu Protocol immediately once it launches on mainnet.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/1c690f62-2fef-442c-b0b5-0f534cbd0b26/b3441a60-cc2d-48b8-a981-55b2fdf74023.png

About:

### **Problem**

Most Sui wallets still feel like developer tools. Users install a browser extension, manage seed phrases, and approve every action through popups that break on mobile and confuse non-crypto users. AI agents make this worse: teams either paste private keys into agent runtimes or block automation entirely. Existing Sui dApps built with Wallet Standard expect a wallet the user already trusts, but extension wallets create friction at connect, sign, and send. There is no simple way to use Sui in real apps, real agents, and real everyday approval flows without giving up self-custody or rebuilding the whole UX from scratch.

### **Solution**

WallCard is a self-custodial Sui wallet in card form. Users connect and sign with the same mental model they already know from banking: card number, CVV, PIN, and step-up approval. For Sui, WallCard plugs into existing dApps through the Sui Wallet Standard and SDK methods like `sui_signPersonalMessage` and `sui_signTransaction`. dApps keep their current integration patterns. Users get a premium approval flow instead of another extension popup. AI agents can request signatures without holding keys. Large moves escalate to push approval on the WallCard mobile app.

### **Technical Implementation**

WallCard uses a card-native signing stack built for production Sui flows:

- **SessionConnect** links a user's Sui address to a dApp origin for 15 minutes after card verification (PAN, CVV, PIN). Repeat visits skip re-entering card details while the session is valid.

- **OnetimeConnect** handles each signing or sending action with a short-lived approval request, email OTP, and optional mobile push approval.

- **Sui Wallet Standard bridge** auto-registers WallCard in the browser so dApps using `@mysten/dapp-kit` and similar libraries can discover and connect to it like any native Sui wallet.

- **SDK signing methods** expose `sui_signPersonalMessage`, `sui_signTransaction`, and Wallet Standard aliases (`sui:signPersonalMessage`, `sui:signTransaction`) through one provider object.

- **Intent-based signing** builds correct Sui intent bytes server-side before Privy raw signing, so personal messages and transactions broadcast cleanly on testnet and mainnet.

- **Agent-safe signing policy** routes low-risk agent requests through email OTP and high-value moves through slide-to-approve push flows on device.

- **Activity and audit layer** records every request origin, method, approval path, and outcome in the WallCard app.

This lets Sui dApps, SDK integrations, and agent workflows share one approval model without changing how transactions are built on chain.

### **Key Features**

- Sui-native signing for personal messages and transactions

- Sui Wallet Standard compatibility for existing dApps

- SessionConnect for fast return visits without repeated card entry

- OnetimeConnect for per-action sign and send approval

- Card, PIN, and OTP step-up on every sensitive action

- Push slide-to-approve for high-value moves on mobile

- Email OTP signing path for agent and automation workflows

- Self-custodial key custody (no shared signer, no key export to agents)

- `getLinkedAddresses().sui` after SessionConnect or app login

- Works in iframe and popup flows for web dApps

- Full activity feed for agent requests, approvals, and on-chain actions

- SDK playground and docs for integrators shipping Sui support quickly

### **User Flow**

**For existing Sui dApps**

- Integrator adds `@wallcard/sdk-core` and creates `createNoderailsCardProvider()`.

- User opens the dApp and triggers connect or sign.

- WallCard opens SessionConnect once. User enters card details and PIN.

- dApp receives the linked Sui address and can call Wallet Standard or SDK sign methods.

- Each sign or send opens OnetimeConnect. User approves with OTP or mobile push.

- Signature or transaction result returns to the dApp. No extension install required.

**For AI agents**

- Agent proposes a Sui message sign or transaction through WallCard APIs.

- WallCard creates a signing request tied to the user and request origin.

- Routine actions approve via email OTP without exposing private keys to the agent.

- Large or sensitive moves push to the WallCard app for slide-to-approve.

- User confirms on device. WallCard signs and returns the result.

- Activity feed logs what the agent requested, approved, and executed.

**For sends and transactions**

- User or dApp builds a Sui transaction with `@mysten/sui`.

- Serialized transaction bytes go to WallCard through `sui_signTransaction`.

- User reviews the action in the approval UI.

- WallCard signs intent bytes and returns signature material for broadcast or execution.

- Transaction completes on Sui with the user's self-custodial address.

### **Why SessionConnect Matters for Sui**

Without SessionConnect, every dApp interaction starts cold. Users re-enter card details, drop off before signing, and agents cannot maintain a stable linked identity per origin.

With SessionConnect, a Sui address stays linked to the dApp for 15 minutes after one card verification. Connect feels instant on return visits. Signing and sending stay gated by OnetimeConnect, so speed does not trade away security. dApps behave like normal Sui apps. Users stay in a card-native flow they already understand.

### **Why WallCard Matters for Sui Teams**

Sui apps should not force users to become wallet experts. Agent products should not require private keys in prompts or memory stores. WallCard gives both groups the same answer: a self-custodial Sui wallet that works inside existing dApps, supports Wallet Standard discovery, and adds card-grade approval for humans and agents alike.
