About:

### **Problem**

Merchants who want to accept crypto, especially on Six, still stitch together too many tools. Checkout, subscriptions, customer records, invoicing, disputes, refunds, and fiat settlement usually live in different products. Browser wallet flows break on public RPCs. Subscription billing needs custom Move contracts and gas sponsorship. Buyers have little protection after they pay. Finance teams still want email invoices, customer history, and a clear path from on-chain revenue to a bank account without paying heavy conversion and payout fees.

### **Solution**

NodeRails is a crypto-native payments platform with **Sui at the center**. Merchants run checkout, subscriptions, invoicing, customer tracking, dispute protection, and bank settlement from one stack. Customers pay with Sui wallets on hosted checkout. Funds sit in on-chain escrow until timelocks clear. Merchants get virtual fiat accounts to receive and route money, then settle to a linked bank account at competitive fees. Sui is not a bolt-on chain. It is a first-class path for one-time capture, sponsored gas, and subscription wallets.

### **Technical Implementation**

NodeRails uses a hybrid architecture:

- **On-chain (Sui):** `NodeRailsEscrow` Move package for capture, settle, refund, dispute resolution, and **NodeRailsWallet** for subscription authorization and pull payments.

- **MTXM:** Multichain transaction manager for sponsored PTBs, platform-signed captures, and subscription renewals without a new user signature every cycle.

- **Server:** Checkout sessions, authorize/capture, webhooks, invoice email delivery, customer accounts, dispute workflows, and payout orchestration.

- **Payment UI:** Hosted checkout at `pay.noderails.com` with Sui wallet connect, same-origin RPC proxy, subscription fund-and-authorize, and intent status polling.

- **Merchant dashboard:** Apps, chains, settlement addresses, **customer directory**, **invoice create and send**, **virtual bank accounts**, and **settle to bank** configuration.

Dispute and refund logic follows escrow timelocks: capture, dispute window, settlement, with on-chain refund and dispute resolution where the chain supports it.

### **Key Features**

**Sui payments**

- Native Sui checkout (testnet and mainnet) with wallet connect and balance reads

- Gas-sponsored wallet setup and capture via MTXM

- NodeRailsWallet subscriptions: fund pool, merchant rule, max per charge, budget cap

- Server-side subscription capture without repeated payer approval within the rule

**Commerce and customers**

- Track customers by email and profile across checkouts, links, and invoices

- Create invoices in the dashboard and **send payment links by email** in one click

- Payment links, checkout sessions, and subscription billing on one model

**Trust and protection**

- **Built-in chargebacks and disputes** with a defined dispute window after capture

- **Refunds** through escrow when eligible

- Customer dispute portal with OTP verification and payment history

- Funds held in escrow until settlement timelocks pass

**Fiat and treasury**

- **Virtual bank accounts** in major currencies (USD, EUR, GBP, and more) for receiving and routing

- **Settle to bank** to your linked account (local and international options)

- Transparent, **low-fee** settlement path from crypto capture to fiat payout

**Operations**

- Webhooks for payment and subscription events

- Merchant dashboard for apps, chains, payouts, and bank

- Docs and demo apps (e.g. buy-me-coffee) for Sui-first integration

### **User Flow**

- Merchant creates an app, configures Sui chain (escrow, registries, settlement address), and links **virtual bank** and **settle-to-bank** details.

- Merchant adds **customers**, sends **invoices by email**, or shares payment links and subscription checkouts.

- Customer pays on Sui: connect wallet, authorize subscription wallet if needed, complete capture.

- Payment is **CAPTURED** in escrow; customer can **raise a dispute** during the dispute window if needed.

- After timelocks, funds **settle**; merchant can **refund** or resolve disputes per policy.

- Merchant routes balance through **virtual accounts** and **settles to bank** at low fees.

- **Customer record** stays linked for repeat purchases, invoices, and support.

### **Why Sui-Centric NodeRails Matters**

Without a unified stack, Sui merchants patch RPC proxies, Move integrations, and subscription logic while still using separate tools for invoices, customers, disputes, and bank payouts.

With NodeRails, **Sui checkout, subscription wallets, buyer protection, customer tracking, invoice email, and bank settlement** work as one product. Merchants ship Sui payments faster. Buyers get clear wallet UX and real dispute protection. Finance gets a straight line from on-chain revenue to the bank at fees that stay low end to end.

About:

**TL;DR: **IFÁ Labs is a **multichain oracle infrastructure** that delivers reliable, real-time price data for RWA assets, especially in emerging markets.

We built an oracle-powered DeFi infrastructure network on base, now on Sui - bringing live price oracle, an oracle-priced swap and sweep protocol, and a multi-asset test token faucet.

Today, Sui's DeFi ecosystem is expanding faster than its supporting infrastructure. New native tokens, emerging market assets, and real-world assets are launching on Sui without the on-chain pricing, swapping, and testing infrastructure that builders need to actually use them.

IFÁ Labs answers three separate but connected problems on Sui:

- Who is pricing this asset on-chain?

- How do I move value between any two assets on Sui without heavy slippage or fragmented gas costs?

- Where do I even get test tokens for this asset to start building?"

By combining a live oracle layer, an oracle-priced swap and sweep protocol, and a developer-first faucet - all built on Sui's object model and Programmable Transaction Blocks - IFÁ Labs provides the quite missing infrastructure layer underneath Sui's growing asset ecosystem.

**PROBLEM**

Sui's DeFi ecosystem faces three distinct infrastructure gaps that compound each other.

- Sui's DeFi ecosystem is growing rapidly - new native tokens, emerging market assets, and real-world assets (RWAs) are launching continuously. All of these require on-chain oracles to price them accurately. Without a reliable price feed, an asset cannot be safely collateralized, swapped at fair value, or integrated into any serious DeFi protocol. Sui's existing oracle coverage has not kept pace with the rate of new asset issuance, leaving a growing share of the ecosystem unpriced.

- The newly launched USDSui is a fast-growing asset on the Sui ecosystem. To promote real adoption, USDSui needs infrastructure that supports interoperability between it and every other asset on Sui - not just isolated liquidity pools, but a swap layer that treats USDSui as a true settlement asset across the ecosystem. Separately, users accumulate fragmented small-value token balances across multiple assets - amounts individually too small to swap economically. Today there is no way to consolidate these balances without paying gas N times over, once per token, which often costs more than the dust itself is worth.

- Most native assets on the Sui ecosystem have no platform where developers can easily obtain test tokens. Builders wanting to integrate against USDSui, SUI, WAL, or other Sui-native assets are left deploying their own mock tokens or skipping integration testing entirely - slowing down ecosystem-wide development.

**SOLUTION**

IFÁ Labs solves all three problems with one connected infrastructure stack, each piece reinforcing the others.

Price Oracle: Live, on-chain PriceFeed shared objects for Sui-native and emerging assets, continuously updated and resolvable by any contract through the OracleRegistry.

Swap & Sweep Protocol: IFÁ SWAP uses the oracle to execute interoperable, oracle-priced swaps between any two supported assets — including USDSui — with zero pool-based slippage. Asset Sweep extends this further: it scans a wallet, prices every token balance using the same oracle, and consolidates everything below a dust threshold into a single asset, atomically, in one Programmable Transaction Block.

Test Token Faucet: A single platform to request testnet tokens for every asset IFÁ Labs supports, removing the barrier that stops developers from testing against real Sui-native assets.

Each product depends on the one before it: the Oracle prices the assets, the Swap and Sweep protocol moves and consolidates them at that price, and the Faucet gets developers the tokens to test all of it.

**WHY SUI**

Sui's object-centric architecture is the reason all three products can work together as one system rather than three disconnected tools.

A PriceFeed is a shared object any contract can reference directly, making oracle data a composable on-chain primitive. IFÁ SWAP reads that same object to price every trade. Asset Sweep reads it again, across every token in a wallet, to compute a sweep — and then uses Programmable Transaction Blocks to execute every resulting swap leg atomically in a single transaction.

This last part is only possible on Sui. On an EVM chain, sweeping five dust tokens into one asset means five separate transactions, five gas payments, and no guarantee they all succeed together. On Sui, it is one PTB: fully atomic, one gas fee, no partial state if anything fails.

About:

AutoYield is an autonomous DeFAI treasury manager that solves the real problem behind DeFi underperformance: cognitive overhead, not yield availability.

Sui currently offers 8-14% annual yields across Scallop, DeepBook, and Cetus. But capturing them requires monitoring three protocols simultaneously, rebalancing manually when APYs shift, understanding impermanent loss math and order book mechanics, and not panic-selling when markets drop.
95% of users do none of this. They deposit into one protocol, forget about it, and watch active managers outperform by 3x to 5x.

AutoYield removes that barrier entirely. Users sign in with Google via zkLogin - no wallet, no seed phrase. They deposit USDC via sponsored transactions- no gas fees.

An AI agent handles everything else forever. The agent polls Scallop, DeepBook, and Cetus every 30 seconds alongside Pyth price feeds.
When risk-adjusted yield improvement exceeds gas cost by 10x, it constructs a Programmable Transaction Block that withdraws, rebalances, and redeposits across all three protocols in one atomic transaction.

If any step fails, everything reverts. This composability is impossible on EVM. Every decision is explained in plain English and stored on Walrus via MemWal, creating a verifiable audit trail of what the agent did and why. Share calculations use OpenZeppelin's audited mul_div library, the same math securing $35 trillion in on-chain value. Nine deterministic guardrails operate off-chain and on-chain- the AI suggests, but contracts enforce hard limits on concentration, drawdown, and rebalance frequency.

We built AutoYield because the future of DeFi is not more dashboards. It is intelligent infrastructure that manages money while humans sleep.

About:

Arc Pay: peer-to-peer crypto marketplace on Sui

Arc Pay is a peer-to-peer marketplace: think OLX or Craigslist, settled on Sui. A seller lists an item and generates a QR code; a buyer scans it and either pays the full price instantly or submits their own private offer at a lower price. Funds are locked in an on-chain escrow until settlement, so neither side has to trust the other: the buyer's money can't be taken until the deal closes, and the seller knows the funds are really there.

What makes it different is that offers are sealed: each buyer's bid is visible only to that buyer and the seller, never to other buyers. There is no public order book to anchor to or snipe, so people bid their honest price instead of racing the lowest visible number. The seller can hand-pick the offers they like, or set an auto-accept rule that automatically settles the best bids the moment they clear a price the seller chooses.

Why it matters: peer-to-peer trade runs on trust that constantly gets abused, with no-shows, "send first" scams, and reversed payments. Arc Pay replaces that trust with on-chain escrow and sealed-bid price discovery: sellers see real demand, and buyers get a guarantee their funds can't be redirected until they get what they paid for.

How it's built

Smart contract (Move): a custom escrow and offer program. Listings, full-price buys, sealed counter-offers, and settlement are all on-chain. Escrow is held in a Move object and can only ever be routed to the seller the buyer chose or refunded to the buyer: a backend authority can settle accepted offers (and run auto-accept) through a constrained admin_settle entry, but it can never redirect funds, because the payout targets come from the escrow object itself.
Indexer: a custom indexer watches the contract's events (offer created, bought, accepted, canceled, refunded), decodes them, and forwards them to the backend so off-chain state (listings, private offers, settlement status) stays in sync with the chain in real time.

App: a React and TypeScript front end using dynamic.xyz for wallet auth: users log in with a Sui wallet (Slush) or email (embedded wallet), scan and generate QR codes, and manage listings and offers. Supabase backs the off-chain database, realtime updates, and the edge functions that custody the backend settlement key.

About:

TrustFlow is a payment rail for money with conditions attached. Instead of trusting a person, a platform, or a multisig to hold funds until work is verified, you lock stablecoins on-chain against milestones. When the work lands, a single signature releases the payment, splits the platform fee, and mints a wallet-native receipt all inside one Sui Programmable Transaction Block (PTB). It's built for freelance and contractor payouts, milestone projects, marketplace escrow, payroll, and any B2B payment where "pay only when the condition is met" matters.

Conditional payments still run on trust nobody actually has.

`- A custodian holds the money. A platform, escrow agent, or marketplace sits between the two parties. It's slow, takes a cut, and can freeze or seize funds, a single point of failure and a single point of censorship.`

`- The steps are fragmented. Approving the work, calculating and routing the platform fee, paying the worker, and producing proof of payment are four` `separate manual actions.`

`- On-chain attempts don't fix it. Most on-chain "escrows" just move the custodian into a multisig or an admin key that can still take the funds, settle over many transactions, and leave no clean, verifiable receipt. And making everything public exposes sensitive dispute details that should stay private.`

TrustFlow collapses the entire flow into one atomic transaction and removes the custodian.

1. Atomic-or-nothing settlement. The hero call, settlement::approve_and_settle, approves the milestone, computes the platform fee in basis points, splits the typed Balance<CoinType>, and returns (net_coin, fee_coin, PaymentReceipt). The PTB then dispatches them in the same transaction: net amount to the

worker, fee to the treasury, receipt to the worker. It either all happens or none of it does no half-finished payouts.

2. No admin key, no custodian. Authorization is object-owned capabilities — ClientCap, ApproverCap, ResolverCap. Only the holder of the right cap can

act, and no god-mode admin can touch the locked funds. The money cannot be frozen or seized by a platform.

3. Public settlement, private evidence. Settlement is public and verifiable on Sui, so anyone can audit that the worker was paid the correct amount. But dispute evidence stays encrypted on Walrus, with decryption gated by Seal to authorized parties keyed on the live dispute state. You get auditability and confidentiality at the same time. the combination compliance and privacy both require.

4. Proof by default. Every settled milestone mints a PaymentReceipt object transferred to the worker an on-chain, verifiable proof of payment, not an

email. The receipt is the record, which kills reconciliation disputes.

5. Dispute path built in. If work is contested, a ResolverCap holder resolves it — full or partial release/refund with the sealed evidence available only to the right parties, and a verifiable outcome recorded on-chain.

About:

### Arthavest — Invest in Indonesian MSMEs. Profits flow per second.

### 🎯 TL;DR

Arthavest is a **two-layer product** combining a reusable DeFi primitive (SuiStream) with an RWA marketplace for Indonesian MSMEs. Investors buy fractional shares of verified businesses (coffee shops, laundry, catering, etc.) and receive monthly profit streams directly to their wallet — every second, atomically, with zero platform fees.

### 😰 The Problem

Indonesia has **64 million MSMEs** contributing **60% of GDP**, but **87% can't access bank loans**. Retail investors lack small-ticket investment rails into local businesses. Traditional crowdfunding charges 10–20% platform fees with manual monthly transfers. There is no real-time, fractional, low-fee investment vehicle connecting investors to MSMEs — until now.

### 💡 The Solution

### Layer 1 — SuiStream Primitive (Reusable DeFi)

A composable money-stream Move module. Each stream is an **object owned by the recipient**, transferable, fractionalizable, and atomic via PTBs. Designed to be reused for payroll, vesting, subscriptions, and micro-installments — Arthavest is its first use case.

### Layer 2 — Arthavest Marketplace App

A marketplace where investors buy fractional shares of verified Indonesian MSMEs and receive monthly profit streams directly to their wallet every second via SuiStream.

### End-to-end flow

- MSME owner onboards → Mint UMKM NFT + share tokens (ARTHA) via PTB

- Investor buys shares → PTB atomic (pay SUI + receive shares + mint receipt)

- Owner reports profit → Walrus proof upload (immutable blob ID)

- DAO jury verifies → 5 jurors, ≥3 approve

- Distribution → PTB bulk `create_stream` for all holders (1 atomic tx for 47–312 investors)

- Investor withdraws → anytime, sub-second finality, sponsored tx for first-timers

### ⚡ Sui-Native Architecture

Every Sui feature used solves a real problem — not a gimmick.

Sui FeatureUsed InBenefit**Object-Centric Model**MSME NFT, ARTHA share token, Stream ObjectDirect ownership without registry mappings**Programmable Transaction Blocks**Bulk profit distribution, buy + mint receiptAtomic, gas-efficient, no partial failure**zkLogin**Investor & MSME onboarding (Google/Twitch)Web2 UX, web3 security — no wallet install**Sponsored Transactions**First-time buy & withdrawZero gas barrier for new users**Walrus Storage**MSME document verification (Akta, NPWP, financials)Immutable audit trail, permanent blob ID**DeepBook**Secondary market for MSME sharesLiquidity without exit window, on-chain orderbook

### Comparison: Arthavest vs EVM equivalent

-

**Key Move structs:**

- `UMKM` (NFT object) — represents an MSME

- `ARTHA` (fungible token) — fractional shares

- `ProfitReport` (shared object) — verified monthly profit with Walrus blob ID

- `Distribution` (NFT receipt) — bulk distribution proof

- `Stream<T>` (SuiStream primitive) — money stream object owned by recipient

###

- Real Sui wallet connection via `@mysten/dapp-kit`

- Supports Sui Wallet, Ethos, Slush (any registered wallet)

- Real on-chain transactions on Sui Testnet (buy shares, distribute profit, withdraw, onboard, vote)

- Toast notifications with "View on Sui Explorer" action button linking to real tx digests

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/32d2fcab-a5fa-47f1-84ca-fdad66a1f5f9/51f4331c-0d27-40fe-9cfd-4fc20cd3e84d.png

About:

**Paystreamer** is an on-chain plugin for Web3 platforms that handles recurring payments without users losing control of their accounts.

**The Pain Point**

Last year, my teammate and I built an award-winning project for the Walrus hackathon called Fundsui, a podcast platform where users could subscribe to creators using crypto. It was an amazing concept, but we hit a massive roadblock: handling recurring payments on-chain was incredibly difficult. Current Web3 solutions are flawed: they force users to manually sign transactions every 30 days (causing massive churn), lock up huge sums of capital in escrow, or drain funds at a per-second rate via payment streaming, which is terrible UX for standard SaaS. We needed a fully decentralized, interval-based solution. That’s why we built PayStreamer.

**The Solution**

PayStreamer is an on-chain, token-agnostic recurring payment protocol for Web3 platforms. It mimics the seamless UX of a Web2 credit card subscription without users ever losing custody of their accounts. Users own and manage a single subscription account across all platforms, granting access to funds via highly customizable, time-based "Policies".

By leveraging OpenZeppelin's rate_limiter library, these policies strictly enforce how much and how often a platform can withdraw funds (e.g., Platform X can withdraw 10 Tokens, but only every 30 days). Users keep their capital completely liquid in their own wallets right up until the moment a payment is triggered.

**How it Works (The Core Protocol)**

Platforms integrate with PayStreamer directly on-chain for zero-middleman settlement. But how does the contract know when to pull funds without a centralized server running cron jobs? We solved this using a network of permissionless "time keepers" called Schedulers. When a billing interval hits, anyone running a Scheduler can call the process_payment function. The Schedulers never hold the funds; they simply execute the smart contract logic based on the user's policy.

**The Live Demo Experience**

To prove how frictionless this protocol can be, we built a Live Demo platform. In our demo, we utilized Sui's sponsored transactions and a custom testnet stablecoin (PUSD). This allows a user to subscribe to a service without needing native gas tokens, demonstrating that PayStreamer can provide a Web2-level onboarding experience while remaining entirely decentralized under the hood. Platforms currently have a volume limit of 1M PUSD every 30 days, causing schedulers to skip platforms that reach this limit and focus on newer platforms.

**Market Opportunity**

PayStreamer bridges the gap between Web3 infrastructure and Web2 business models. Our immediate target audiences include:

- Web3 SaaS & Tooling: RPC providers, analytics dashboards, and node infrastructure that require stable Monthly Recurring Revenue (MRR).

- Decentralized Content Platforms: Substack or Patreon competitors (like our previous project, Fundsui), looking for native crypto monetization.

- DAOs: Automating recurring grants, continuous contributor funding, or software subscriptions.

**Next Steps**

The core protocol works on Testnet. Our next step is to reach out to existing Web3 content platforms and DAOs to understand how we can tailor this plugin to their exact integration needs. Moving toward mainnet, we plan to invest heavily in smart contract security audits and implement on-chain financial incentives for our decentralized Schedulers. I'm currently a full-time student, but I am fully committed to taking on the challenge of scaling PayStreamer into a foundational Web3 payment layer.

About:

### Why We Built [Scooba.live](https://scooba-live-app.vercel.app/)

**The Problem** Current DeFi yield strategies demand relentless manual effort. Users are constantly forced to claim, swap, re-deposit, and rebalance their assets. Furthermore, the ecosystem lacks a seamless bridge to connect everyday fiat income with the power of on-chain wealth generation.

**The Solution** [Scooba.live](https://scooba-live-app.vercel.app/) transforms a standard fiat paycheck into an auto-compounding, on-chain portfolio through a simple, one-time setup. Users can easily define their risk profile and asset allocation via our intuitive strategy-builder UI. From there, our keeper bot takes over, automating scheduled DCA (Dollar-Cost Averaging) and continuously auto-compounding yields. The vault supports direct crypto deposits as well as recurring fiat charges seamlessly integrated via Stripe.

**The Sui Technology Advantage** We leverage the unique architectural strengths of the Sui Network to power our platform:

- **Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTBs):** We utilize PTBs to bundle complex, multi-step operations, such as splitting funds, minting tokens, and supplying liquidity, executing them completely atomically in a single transaction.

- **Sui Object Model:** The entire investment position is represented as a composable, on-chain Share Token. Relying on Sui's object-centric architecture, these tokens guarantee type-safe ownership, ensuring that user positions remain fully modular and interoperable across the broader DeFi ecosystem.

About:

**InvoFi is a non-custodial invoice financing protocol on Sui.** Unpaid invoices become tradeable Receivable Objects, and because ownership of the payment right lives on-chain, the same invoice physically can't be sold to two different financiers at once.

**THE PROBLEM**

Invoice factoring today runs entirely on trust. There's no shared record of who actually owns the right to get paid, so the same invoice can quietly get sold twice. The supporting paperwork sits in someone's email, so a buyer has no real way to check it before they wire money. And if a payer defaults or disputes delivery, there's no rule that actually fires — just whoever argues harder afterward.

**WHAT WE BUILT**

We put invoice ownership and state directly on Sui. An issuer mints a Receivable Object for the invoice, and the payer has to acknowledge it on-chain before it's even eligible to be listed for financing — that alone kills a lot of the fraud vector. Once a buyer purchases the payment right, the contract updates who's actually owed the money, so settlement always finds the current owner instead of whoever issued the invoice originally. We also push the invoice and its metadata to Walrus with a checksum attached, so a buyer can verify the evidence themselves instead of taking our word for it.

On top of that we built two escrow modes, both enforced in Move:

A security deposit option, where the payer puts up USDC as collateral. Pay on time and it comes back automatically. Default past the grace period and the buyer can claim it.

A full settlement escrow, where the payer locks the entire invoice amount upfront. Confirm delivery and the funds release to whoever currently holds the payment right. Miss the deadline and it refunds automatically — nobody has to chase anyone down.

**LIVE PROOF**

This isn't just a contract sitting unused — the whole flow works end to end on Sui Testnet right now: create, acknowledge, list, buy, pay, all of it. Every step has a real transaction digest, and evidence uploads actually land on Walrus with real blob IDs you can pull up yourself. We also added an optional gasless mode, so if you don't want to hold SUI you don't have to — a sponsor wallet covers gas and you only need USDC.

**STACK**

Sui Move for the receivable and escrow logic, Walrus for evidence storage, Cloudflare Pages Functions sitting in front of the index so nothing gets written until it's verified on-chain, and a React/TypeScript frontend on top.

About:

Conduit is programmable stablecoin treasury settlement infrastructure on Sui. A team, protocol, or DAO creates a shared Treasury<T>, funds it with a payment asset, and authors recurring or one-shot PaymentRule objects for payroll, vendor payouts, subscriptions, or revenue splits. Once a rule is due, anyone can settle it; Conduit's reference operator runs as a GitHub Actions cron, discovers due rules from Sui events, uploads a signed receipt to Walrus, and submits the settlement transaction; so payouts happen with no backend or machine you babysit.

Every payout emits a PaymentExecuted event carrying the Walrus blob id, so the payment record is independently retrievable and tamper-evident; payroll, vendor, and revenue-share payouts become auditable by anyone, not just by trusting a database row. The live testnet proof shows a real Circle USDC rule settled via execute_payout<...::usdc::USDC>, the payee receiving USDC, and the corresponding Walrus receipt resolving publicly.

Conduit also includes a DeepBook settlement-time FX module: execute_payout_swapped<Out,T> converts the treasury asset into the payee's preferred asset atomically through a DeepBook v3 pool before transfer, in the same transaction. The owner console supports treasury creation, deposits, rule authoring, and a live public payments feed; the operator and Move package are fully reproducible from the README, and CI builds the Move package, agent, and UI on every push.

Live on Sui testnet. Verify in 30 seconds, no wallet needed: the landing page shows the latest settled payout with links to the on-chain tx and the Walrus receipt.

About:

**Hongbao — physical gift cards that put crypto into real people's hands**

**What it is**
Hongbao is a physical Bluetooth card that lets anyone hand crypto to a real person in real life. The recipient connects to the card from their phone, completes a quick task if one is set, and the token lands straight in their own Sui wallet — no middleman ever holds the funds.

**The problem**
Distribution is where crypto's two hardest problems meet. Online airdrops and quests get drained by sybil farms — projects spend more filtering bots than reaching users, and real people walk away with thin rewards. And for newcomers, "set up a wallet first" is a wall most never cross. There's no simple, physical, trustless way to put a token in someone's hand.

**How it works**
Each card is a one-time signing device. A private key is generated and sealed inside the card's secure-element chip (secp256k1), never leaves it, signs a single claim, and is then spent — nothing to leak. Rewards sit in an immutable Move contract with no admin keys and release directly to whoever claims the card. A relayer sponsors gas, so the recipient needs zero crypto to claim. We never custody funds or hold anyone's keys.

**What we built for Overflow**
A Sui-native Hongbao: Move contracts for locking and claiming rewards, the card-to-Sui signing flow over Bluetooth, and a working end-to-end demo where a physical card delivers a Sui asset to a real wallet.

**Why it matters**
For projects, it's a sybil-proof, fully measurable way to put tokens into real users' hands at events — real humans, real attribution, budget that reaches the people you actually wanted. Longer term, it's a physical on-ramp bringing everyday people from Web2 into Web3.

About:

### Rivio Project Submission

### What is Rivio?

Rivio is a neobank built on Sui. It lets you hold, send, swap, and borrow crypto all from an interface that looks and feels like a regular banking app.

There's no crypto jargon, no seed phrases, and no setup. You sign in with Google, and your wallet is created automatically in the background. That's it.

We built Rivio for a simple reason: crypto is powerful, but using it still feels like work. Too many apps, too much terminology, too much friction. Rivio strips all of that away and gives you something that just works. Fast payments, real yields, and smart money tools wrapped in an experience anyone can pick up in under a minute.

### What you can do with it

**Send:** move USDC or SUI to anyone, anywhere, in a few taps. No bank forms, no waiting days.

**Receive:** share your QR code or address and get paid instantly.

**Swap:** exchange tokens at the best available rate, routed through the 7K aggregator.

**Borrow:** supply your assets as collateral and borrow against them through Navi Protocol, one of Sui's leading lending markets.

**AI assistant:** ask questions in plain English, check your spending, and trigger actions just by typing. No menus needed.

**Virtual card:** a Mastercard-branded card tied to your wallet. Rain Cards integration is in progress and launches before mainnet.

**USD and GBP accounts:** virtual fiat accounts for moving money in and out. Also coming via Rain Cards.

### How the tech works (simply put)

When you sign in with Google, Sui's zkLogin system quietly creates a real self-custodial wallet tied to your identity. No seed phrase is generated. No private key is handed to you or stored by us. Your wallet is mathematically derived from your Google account, which means it's yours, always, even if Rivio stops existing.

Transactions are sponsored, meaning users never need to hold SUI just to pay gas fees. You tap confirm, and it goes through. The complexity lives in the infrastructure, not the interface.

Swaps run through the 7K Protocol aggregator, which finds the best available rate across Sui's liquidity pools. Lending is powered by Navi Protocol. Prices are fetched live. The backend is built in Python using FastAPI, with a PostgreSQL database and a Next.js frontend.

### Who it's for

Rivio is for anyone who sends money internationally, holds crypto, or just wants a smarter account. You don't need to know what a blockchain is to use it. If you've used Revolut or CashApp, Rivio will feel immediately familiar, except your money moves faster, costs less, and is actually yours.

### What's live and what's coming

Everything core to the product is built and working. A few features are waiting on mainnet access or active partnerships to finalise.

Feature Status Note Send USDC / SUI Ready Waiting on mainnet Swap tokens Ready Waiting on mainnet liquidity Borrow / supply via Navi Ready Waiting on mainnet Transaction history Ready Waiting on live on-chain data AI assistant Partial UI done, full action execution coming Virtual Mastercard In progress Rain Cards launches before mainnet USD virtual account In progress Rain Cards launches before mainnet GBP virtual account In progress Rain Cards launches before mainnet Physical card Planned Post-mainnet $RIVIO token Planned Tokenomics in progress

### A note on ownership

Your wallet belongs to you, not Rivio. We never hold your keys or control your funds. Everything is derived from your Google identity through Sui's zkLogin, which means your assets live on-chain and stay accessible no matter what. We just built the interface on top.

That's the version of crypto we think more people deserve access to. Fast, clean, and actually yours.

About:

Problem :
Organizations, DAOs, startups, creators, and on-chain businesses increasingly manage capital across wallets, protocols, treasuries, payroll systems, and investment platforms. Today, treasury operations remain fragmented. Payments are static transfers, treasury policies are enforced manually, obligations are tracked off-chain, and capital deployment decisions require constant human intervention. Funds often sit idle while teams manually coordinate reserves, payroll, investments, liquidity management, and risk controls across multiple tools.

As organizations adopt DeFi, the problem becomes even larger. Capital can be deployed into lending, liquidity, and yield strategies, but there is no unified system that continuously protects obligations, enforces treasury policies, evaluates risk, manages reserves, and automates capital movement. Financial operations become a collection of disconnected transactions instead of a programmable financial workflow.

Solution :

TITAN solves this problem by transforming treasury management, payments, and capital deployment into programmable financial systems built on Sui.

At its core, TITAN introduces programmable treasuries that allow organizations, creators, DAOs, startups, and on-chain businesses to define objectives, obligations, reserve requirements, risk limits, allocation policies, and execution rules directly on chain. Instead of managing funds manually, users create financial mandates that govern how capital should move, be protected, and be deployed over time.

Built on Sui Move and Programmable Transaction Blocks, TITAN enables treasury controlled payroll, revenue routing, investment mandates, treasury preservation strategies, guardian enforced controls, and automated capital allocation through a unified financial operating system. Multiple financial actions can be executed atomically, allowing payments, allocations, and treasury operations to function as programmable workflows rather than isolated transactions.

The platform includes a Liquidity Terminal that aggregates opportunities across the Sui ecosystem, providing liquidity discovery, market depth analysis, yield monitoring, portfolio visibility, and deployment pathways into external protocols. TITAN integrates with protocols such as Navi, Scallop, and Cetus to support lending, liquidity provision, yield generation, and capital deployment strategies within treasury workflows.

Smart Wallet Rules allow users to automate financial behavior through policy driven execution. Treasuries can automatically allocate capital, trigger investments, enforce spending limits, react to risk conditions, execute payroll linked actions, or perform proof linked financial operations without requiring continuous manual intervention.

Guardian controls and risk engines continuously evaluate treasury health, reserve coverage, liquidity exposure, allocation concentration, and execution safety before capital is deployed. Treasury actions are governed by programmable rules designed to protect capital while maintaining operational flexibility.

Every workflow produces verifiable execution records through the Proof Center, enabling transaction validation, workflow auditing, portfolio tracking, and proof based verification of financial actions. This creates a transparent system where treasury decisions, capital movements, and execution outcomes can be independently verified.

By combining programmable treasuries, financial workflows, automated rules, liquidity intelligence, portfolio management, proof verification, guardian controls, and DeFi integrations, TITAN transforms payments into programmable financial actions and provides a complete operating system for managing, moving, protecting, and deploying capital on Sui.

About:

Nigeria processed $92.1 billion in on-chain crypto value between July 2024 and June 2025 ; ranking second globally in grassroots crypto adoption. Nigeria is the global leader in stablecoin adoption, with 25.9 million users and an 11.9% penetration rate, accounting for 60% of sub-Saharan Africa's stablecoin inflows since 2019.

And yet Nigerian merchants still don't accept crypto payments.

Not because they don't want to. Because they couldn't. The infrastructure simply didn't exist. Until now. We built it.

**What we built **

Linqswitch is a stablecoin-powered merchant payments suite built on Sui. It gives any Nigerian business the ability to accept crypto payments, generate payment links, create professional invoices, and receive settlement in naira, without any new hardware or technical knowledge

Our services include :

- Scan-to-Pay — merchants display a static QR code, customers pay in USDC or USDsui via any Sui compatible wallet.

- Payment Links — merchants generate and share payment links.

- Invoicing — merchants create and send professional invoices directly from their Linqswitch dashboard

- Instant NGN Settlement — stablecoin payments settle to the merchant's local Nigerian bank account in naira. No crypto knowledge required on the merchant side, they just get paid.

Nigeria has over 41 million SMEs contributing almost 50% to GDP and 80% of national employment. 22 million Nigerians ; 10.3% of the population now hold or use cryptocurrency, 52% of them under 30.

Tens of millions of crypto-holding customers, Millions of businesses that can't accept their payments. That gap is where Linqswitch lives.

Linqswitch is live on Sui and we onboarded a restaurant in Benin City, Nigeria and completed a live stablecoin payment via QR code in under 60 seconds, captured on video and included in this submission. 8 additional businesses across fashion, food and beverage, and e-commerce are on the waitlist with onboarding underway.

**Why Sui**

Stablecoin transfers on Sui are now completely free.

Not cheaper. Free.

That single fact changes everything about building a merchant payments product. Every other chain requires users to hold a separate gas token just to move stablecoins. That means a customer who wants to pay a merchant in USDC first needs to acquire gas fees just to cover the cost of the transaction. That is not a very scalable payments experience.

On Sui, that friction is gone entirely. A customer holds USDC or USDsui, they pay a merchant, the transaction goes through. No gas token. No extra steps or failed transaction because someone forgot to top up their wallet. Nothing standing between the customer and the payment.

When the free stablecoin transfers feature went live on Sui, we knew Linqswitch would be the perfect product to build on top of it.

For a product designed for everyday Nigerian merchants, Sui is the only chain where the stablecoin payment experience is clean enough to work in the real world. Every other chain introduces a layer of complexity that kills adoption before it begins.

Gasless stablecoin transfers are the reason Linqswitch is possible at the scale we're building toward.

The demand is undeniable. What's been missing is the merchant-side infrastructure to complete the loop.

Linqswitch closes that loop. When businesses accept crypto directly, consumers don't need to offramp anymore. The payment loop closes onchain, that is the shift Linqswitch is building on Sui, starting in Nigeria, scaling across Africa.

About:

**SUIPlug — The Future of Electronics E-Commerce
**

SUIPlug is a next-generation Web3 electronics marketplace built on Next.js 16, React 19, and Tailwind CSS. It bridges the gap between Web2 shopping convenience and Web3 digital ownership by providing a seamless, gasless checkout experience that converts every purchase into a secure, tradeable on-chain receipt NFT.

**Key Features & Sui Integrations:**

- **Frictionless Web2 Onboarding (zkLogin):** Users sign in instantly with Google OAuth via a custom `@mysten/wallet-standard` wrapper, bypassing the need for browser extensions or seed phrases.

- **Atomic PTB Checkouts:** Multiple items in a shopping cart are processed in a single, atomic Programmable Transaction Block—splitting payments, routing funds to the merchant, and minting receipt NFTs concurrently.

- **Gas Sponsoring:** Features a server-side gas station that sponsors checkouts, allowing new users to shop without holding SUI.

- **Sui Kiosk Secondary Resale:** Receipt NFTs are minted under a shared Kiosk with a `TransferPolicy` enforcing a 2.5% creator royalty. Users can list and resell their gadgets securely on the secondary market.

- **Decentralized Storage (Walrus):** Product catalogs and custom AI-generated artwork (minted via Google Imagen 4.0 at checkout) are stored directly on the Walrus decentralized blob network.

- **Cryptographic Admin Gating:** The admin inventory panel uses sessionless, timestamped cryptographic signatures to authorize catalog changes.

About:

**Meridian** is an **AI-native perpetual trading platform on Sui** that lets users trade **crypto, commodities, FX, ETFs, and stock-based markets** from one testnet margin account.

Users can trade markets such as **BTC, ETH, SUI, Gold, Oil, EUR/USD, Apple, Tesla, Nvidia, Microsoft, Google, Amazon, SPY, and QQQ**, using **MRDN collateral** and a familiar perps interface.

**Live app:** [https://www.meridian-sui.xyz](https://www.meridian-sui.xyz)
**Docs:** [https://meridian-docs-pi.vercel.app](https://meridian-docs-pi.vercel.app)

**Sui package ID:** `0xbba40bea047bb55a5541f3748d5892de63f34c54b7fa8c5b8006d88e11ab93af`

Meridian combines **Sui Programmable Transaction Blocks**, **live oracle-based pricing**, **Strategy NFTs**, **Conditional PTBs**, **TP/SL automation**, and an **AI-assisted trading flow**. Users can connect a wallet, mint and deposit MRDN, open **long or short perps**, manage positions, review portfolio history, and automate execution through keeper-powered sessions.

A core feature is the **chat trading interface**, where users can type intents like *“short Tesla with 100 MRDN at 2x”* or *“open BTC long when price reaches 65,000”*. Meridian converts that intent into a **reviewable transaction plan**, shows live price estimates, and only executes after wallet approval.

For automation, users can create **Conditional PTBs** and enable delegated keeper sessions so approved rules such as **take-profit**, **stop-loss**, and **price-triggered entries** can execute even when the user is offline, within user-defined limits.

On-chain, Meridian uses **Move contracts** for margin accounts, perp markets, MRDN collateral, strategy objects, conditional orders, funding/skew controls, vault solvency checks, and liquidation paths. Off-chain, the product includes an **indexer and keeper system** for portfolio state, recent activity, funding, conditional execution, and automation monitoring.

*Meridian demonstrates how AI, PTBs, and Sui-native automation can make multi-asset perpetual trading more accessible, programmable, and safer for users.*

About:

**Overview**
AsyncSignals bridges the critical gap between raw blockchain infrastructure and actionable market intelligence. While traditional multi-chain trackers treat high-throughput ecosystems like flat accounting ledgers, AsyncSignals captures deep, state-changing execution layers. Deployed live on Sui Mainnet, the platform introduces a dedicated **Move DeFi Behavior Engine** that moves beyond basic wallet balance transfers to actively decode real-time object mutations across core primitives like DeepBook and Cetus.

**Technical Architecture & Core Integrations**

- **Move-Object Ingestion Layer:** The analytics pipeline is engineered directly around Sui's asset-oriented model. It captures structural smart contract modifications, allowing users to track protocol-level exposure, value flows, and accurate whale net-flows.

- **NodeOps Insight Hub:** To ingestion-heavy data pipelines smoothly across Sui, Solana, and EVM layers, our core data collectors are managed via NodeOps orchestration. This provides validator-grade telemetry, real-time node health indexing, and contextual error handling (e.g., automated gas alerts).

- **Agentic AI Context Layer:** Powered by our background telemetry worker, the platform abstracts cryptographic complexity by translating transaction logs into highly readable, plain-English market snapshots for analysts and developers alike.

**🛠️ Value Provided to the Sui Ecosystem**

- **Deeper Behavioral Analytics:** Brings institutional-grade visibility to Sui's core liquidity primitives (DeepBook, Cetus, Scallop, Navi, and Suilend) by tracking asset mutations rather than just superficial token transfers.

- **Infrastructure Transparency:** Showcases how NodeOps can be utilized to host and monitor high-volume, multi-chain indexing clusters with automated recovery.

- **Mainnet Operational Readiness:** Unlike early-stage concepts, AsyncSignals is fully functional, deployed live on the mainnet, and positioned to drive active developer and analyst engagement immediately.

**🗺️ Future Milestones & Post-Hackathon Roadmap**

- **Milestone 1:** Upgrade the current indexing pipeline to track complex collateral ratio updates across Sui lending markets and hook up automated Telegram/Discord notification webhooks.

- **Milestone 2:** Launch an open API endpoint emitting a comprehensive JSON graph mapping cross-protocol liquidity migration patterns per epoch.

- **Milestone 3:** Embed historical logs using Oracle 26AI Native Vector Search to allow developers to perform natural-language queries against historical Sui protocol states.

About:

**X-Market on Sui** is a prediction market product suite on the Sui blockchain. At the foundation, a unified on-chain event engine (Oracle) serves as the single source of truth; on top of it sit two composable modules: **X-Market** offers a parametric AMM with PDF-based pricing, where users buy `Position` tokens with Circle native USDC to trade interval and digital options; **SuiProphet Network** lets prophets publish Seal-encrypted private analysis that subscribers pay to unlock, with post-event on-chain audit of track records. A shared `EventRoot` binds the real-world event, lock time, and settlement outcome—keeping “signal following” and “betting” data in sync instead of siloed. The repository includes Move contracts, a Next.js web app, a Flutter mobile client, and off-chain services such as Indexer and LP Guard, all deployed and verified on Sui Testnet.

About:

Ajo brings a savings, and fundraising tradition used by millions across Nigeria and beyond, also known as Esusu,  fully on-chain. In a savings circle, members contribute a fixed amount each round and the whole pot rotates to one member at a time until everyone has been paid. Traditionally this relies on a human coordinator who holds everyone's money, and every year, coordinators vanish with the pot.

Ajo replaces that coordinator with a Move smart contract on Sui. Members contribute directly to the contract, which holds the funds and automatically pays out the full pot to the next member in rotation. No individual ever holds the money, so no one can abscond, and no one can skip the queue. Every contribution and payout is logged on-chain for full auditability.

Built by a physics student and university investment-society analyst in Lagos, Ajo targets the real-world emerging-market savings problem its builder has lived. v1 removes the coordinator; the roadmap adds staked collateral to eliminate member default, on-chain reputation, and private circles.

About:

Veritas is the first optimistic fraud-proof engine for verifiable AI inference on Sui — opML. A public, deterministic AI judge reads zkTLS-signed evidence and settles an on-chain prediction market with no oracle and no committee. Challenge its verdict and the chain binary-searches the model's ~30M-op run and re-executes ONE micro-op in a ~330-line Move referee, slashing the liar's bond. We turned "prove an AI ran right" from 10,000×-cost zk proofs into nearly free. The market is application #1 — opML is a new Sui primitive: what zkLogin is to identity, opML is to a verifiable AI verdict.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/6fdc0f8b-ec31-432d-9c49-40b60282d01c/0ce0cc7a-d93d-4b9c-8919-4712badadf66.jpg
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/6fdc0f8b-ec31-432d-9c49-40b60282d01c/0bc2f9ca-958e-4fb2-a702-dd7ca9c6af07.jpg

About:

**Payment infrastructure for the agentic economy on Sui** —** **so AI agents can pay and get paid without a human in the loop.

**The problem**

Autonomous agents are about to become the internet's biggest buyers — of data, API calls, compute, and each other's work. But the moment an agent hits a paid resource, it stalls.

**The solution**

Tentacle Pay gives agents a wallet they control, an HTTP-native payment rail ([x402](https://x402.org/)) on Sui, and a single balance they can spend across chains — gasless, non-custodial, and sub-cent.

**Why Sui?**

Tentacle Pay is only possible *because* of what Sui ships

- **Gasless stablecoin transfers** — Sui lets an agent move USDC and other stablecoins **without holding a gas token**, with sub-second finality and parallel execution. The facilitator sponsors gas on top, so an agent needs *only* a stablecoin balance to start paying. This is the difference between "agents need a funded gas wallet on every chain" and "agents just hold USDC" — it's what makes high-frequency, sub-cent agent payments actually work.

- **Ika 2PC-MPC dWallets** — The agent’s funds stay on Sui. When it pays a service on another chain, Tentacle Pay routes the payment through a **dWallet** — a wallet whose private key is split across [*Ika*](https://ika.xyz/)’s 2PC-MPC network, so no single party can sign for it. The dWallet produces a signature valid on the destination chain, funded by the USDC the agent pays on Sui.

**How it works?**

**x402 **— Overview: [https://docs.tentaclepay.com/x402/overview](https://docs.tentaclepay.com/x402/overview)

- Client requests a resource

- Server responds with `402 Payment Required`

- Client signs a payment

- Server verifies with the facilitator

- Server settles and returns the resource

**Cross-chain x402 **— Overview: [https://docs.tentaclepay.com/cross-chain/overview](https://docs.tentaclepay.com/cross-chain/overview)

- The agent holds one Sui wallet

- It pays in Sui stablecoins

- The Ika's dWallet signs authorization for the destination

- The payment settles on the destination

**Wallet (**`tpay`**)**— Overview: [https://docs.tentaclepay.com/wallet/overview](https://docs.tentaclepay.com/wallet/overview)

A command-line wallet built for autonomous agents on Sui.

`tpay` is a wallet your agent holds and spends on its own. It keeps a Sui keypair in your OS keychain, enforces the limits you set, and turns any x402 paywall into a transparent retry.

Install: `curl -fsSL https://tentaclepay.com/install | bash`
Setup: `tpay setup`

CLI Reference: [https://docs.tentaclepay.com/wallet/cli-reference](https://docs.tentaclepay.com/wallet/cli-reference)

**What's live**

- **Wallet (**`tpay`) — create a wallet, and pay for your x402 API

- **Sui x402 SDK **— x402 has been out for years and Sui is arguably the best chain for it, yet no one shipped to the official x402 repo, and we think it's because the usual coin-object gas sponsorship just doesn't fit x402's flow, it forces the sponsor to split a gas coin and hand it to the user, and it can't be signed asynchronously — so we built ours on Sui's new address-balance gas sponsorship, which scales and signs async, and our SDK lets users pay with either a coin object or address balance and handles the choice automatically.`@tentaclepay/sui-x402` implements the `exact` payment scheme for Sui and fully compatible with the official x402.

- **Sui x402 Facilitator** — it verifies signed payment payloads, sponsors gas, and submits the settlement transaction to Sui. URL: [https://facilitator.tentaclepay.com](https://facilitator.tentaclepay.com)

- **Move packages **— The `tentaclepay` Move package signs payment payloads through [*Ika*](https://ika.xyz/) 2PC-MPC dWallets. It exposes `pay_and_sign`, which collects a USDC payment and routes a message to Ika’s `DWalletCoordinator` for threshold signing — gated by a verifier attestation. Testnet Package ID: *0x524cbabd8f4cd78f0913d638d10c101679e31ba4064603f82de6dee4fd59fd44*

**Roadmap**

- Merge our Sui x402 SDK ([https://github.com/tentaclepay/sui-x402](https://github.com/tentaclepay/sui-x402)) — Open pull request to official x402 repo ([https://github.com/x402-foundation/x402](https://github.com/x402-foundation/x402)), make it as a standard x402 mechanism for Sui.

- Discovery Layer — AI agents can autonomously discover, negotiate, and pay for digital services without a human completing a checkout flow.

- ***Intent-based*** model for cross-chain settlement — The next version, we're going to open the protocol for everyone to provide the liquidity across chains. Our contract design are currently running a verifier (act as a single solver) which allow user to settle on destination chain and we're going to open it to let anyone to earn by providing liquidity and take some spread from each settlement.

- Extend the payment rails — Collaborating with MPP team to support MPP on Sui. Integrate it with `tpay`.

**Open-source**

**GitHub**

- **Wallet (tpay):** [https://github.com/tentaclepay/tpay](https://github.com/tentaclepay/tpay)

- **Cross-chain payments (Move package):** [https://github.com/tentaclepay/v1](https://github.com/tentaclepay/v1)

- **TypeScript SDK for cross-chain payment processing:** [https://github.com/tentaclepay/ts-sdk](https://github.com/tentaclepay/ts-sdk)

- **Cross-chain verifier server:** [https://github.com/tentaclepay/verifier](https://github.com/tentaclepay/verifier)

- **Sui x402 SDK:** [https://github.com/tentaclepay/sui-x402](https://github.com/tentaclepay/sui-x402)

- **Apps (Website and x402 Facilitator)**: [https://github.com/tentaclepay/apps](https://github.com/tentaclepay/apps)

- **Docs: **[https://github.com/tentaclepay/docs](https://github.com/tentaclepay/docs)

**Links**

About:

**Adaptive Funding Engine — DeepSurge 2026 | Sui Blockchain**

A fully on-chain milestone-based scholarship and grant disbursement protocol built on Sui. Instead of all-or-nothing payments, funds are released adaptively — recipients earn bonuses for early delivery, lose a percentage per day for late submissions, and withheld funds automatically recycle to the next person in the waitlist.

**What's in this demo:**

- 5 Move smart contract modules (1,291 lines, 27 unit tests) deployed on Sui Testnet

- Manager creates a milestone agreement → funds locked in on-chain escrow

- Recipient submits cryptographic proof of work

- Reviewer votes to approve — quorum triggers adaptive payout

- Funds stream to the recipient via a configurable vesting curve (linear, stepped, or accelerating)

- Waitlist system: Grace Hopper queued and auto-funded when pool allows

- Soulbound credential minted on completion

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/fd5cf58c-bf6f-4cf8-9891-2cc50ee269c8/b7d885e2-0b0e-45ec-9871-f56c5e1825a4.png

About:

**Sui SafeSend** is a decentralized, secure peer-to-peer payments escrow protocol built on the Sui blockchain. It combines reversible transactions and email-based onboarding to eliminate transaction anxiety and drive mass Web2 adoption.

**The Problems We Solve**

1. **Transaction Anxiety & Irreversible Mistakes**: Traditional blockchain transactions are irreversible. A single typo in a long hexadecimal address, or sending to the wrong network, results in permanent loss of funds. This creates massive friction and anxiety for every user.

2. **Web3 Onboarding Friction for Non-Crypto Users**: Forcing non-crypto users to download browser extensions, understand private keys, write down seed phrases, and buy gas tokens (SUI) *before* they can receive any funds is a major barrier to mass adoption.

**The Solution**

Sui SafeSend solves both issues by acting as a secure P2P safety net:

• **Reversible Safety Window**: Sent funds are deposited into an on-chain shared escrow contract. During a configurable safety window (e.g., 1 hour), the sender can instantly cancel the transaction and recall their SUI if they spot a mistake.

• **Instant Email Onboarding**: Send SUI directly to any Google email address. SafeSend deterministically derives their OIDC zkLogin address. The recipient simply logs in using their Google account to claim the funds via passwordless Web2 OAuth, no wallet extension required.

• **Auto-Gas Faucet & Keeper**: A background keeper bot monitors the chain to auto-release expired escrows. Additionally, it detects new zkLogin addresses with pending claims and automatically issues gas SUI, enabling non-crypto recipients to claim their funds for free.

• **0.1% Platform Fee**: The protocol charges a tiny 0.1% platform fee on successfully claimed transfers, routed directly to the platform's treasury.

**Developer Composability & Integration**

SafeSend is designed as an open and composable protocol, allowing other applications and wallets to build on top:

• **dApp Checkout Integration**: E-commerce and dApps can integrate time-locked safety windows directly into their payment flows using our Move targets.

• **Developer Docs Hub**: The application features a built-in, GitBook-style developer panel with copyable, syntax-highlighted code samples for smart contract structures, zkLogin identity parameters, and SDK integration guides.

**How We Leverage Sui's Unique Architecture**

1. **Sui zkLogin (Web2 Onboarding)**: SafeSend resolves the friction of Web3 onboarding by allowing users to send SUI directly to Google email addresses. Non-crypto recipients can claim their funds passwordlessly using Google OAuth.

2. **Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTB)**: All actions from splitting deposit coins to moving them into the time-locked escrow vault, are executed atomically in a single, efficient PTB transaction.

3. **Shared Object Smart Contracts**: Escrow vaults are managed on-chain as Move shared objects, allowing decentralized state management during the safety window.

4. **Event-Driven Keeper Automation & Faucet**: A Node.js keeper bot tracks on-chain events to automatically release finalized escrows and fund gas fees for new zkLogin users, creating a completely gasless experience for the recipient.

**Tech Stack**

• **Move Smart Contracts**: Escrows, time-locks, cancellations, and a 0.1% platform fee.

• **React 18, TypeScript, & Vanilla CSS**: Premium, fully responsive frontend.

• **@mysten/sui TS SDK & @mysten/dapp-kit**: Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTBs) and wallet connector.

• **Vercel Serverless Function Proxy**: Secure zkLogin proving service utilizing **Shinami API**.

• **Node.js**: Event-driven automation keeper bot and gas faucet.

Media:
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/d609f909-aae4-4f61-9d61-fa13f885a548/279d2c19-7205-488d-80cd-2b16b2ad2c3e.png

About:

Tollgate: Trustless HTTP 402 AI Paywall on SUI

AI crawlers scrape the web for free. Publishers have no recourse, robots.txt is ignored, and platform-level paywalls are trivially bypassed. Tollgate enforces access at the protocol layer using Move smart contracts on SUI.

When an AI agent hits a protected route, the server issues an on-chain PaywallChallenge object via the tollgate::paywall Move module and returns HTTP 402. The agent builds a Programmable Transaction Block to call pay_and_unlock(), transferring SUI and consuming the challenge object atomically. Consuming the object is the replay protection, there is no database, no nonce cache, no trusted server state. A second attempt with the same challenge ID fails on-chain because the object no longer exists.

For publishers with revenue-sharing needs, the tollgate::vault module lets them deploy a PublisherVault encoding a configurable split across publisher, content pool, and protocol, routed atomically in a single PTB. On-chain stats accumulate in the vault with no indexer required.

The result is a paywall that is enforced by consensus, not configuration.

About:

Metrom([https://x.com/metromxyz](https://x.com/metromxyz)) is an incentive orchestration platform built to design, launch, and manage on-chain incentive programs. It began with a focus on concentrated AMMs and is now expanding to support a wider range of DeFi primitives including CDPs, lending and borrowing. Metrom helps protocols optimize capital attraction and retention through performance-driven campaigns. Liquidity providers (LPs) can deploy, manage, and earn on their capital.

**What problem we are solving?**

DeFi protocols spend hundreds of millions of dollars each year on liquidity mining and incentive programs to attract liquidity, users, and trading activity. However, most of these rewards are distributed regardless of whether the desired outcomes are achieved.

This often leads to inefficient spending, short-term 'mercenary capital' and poor alignment between protocol goals and reward distribution. Protocols frequently end up paying for liquidity that leaves as soon as incentives end, with limited visibility into the effectiveness of their incentive spend.

Metrom solves this problem through KPI-based incentives. Instead of distributing rewards blindly, protocols can tie rewards to measurable outcomes such as TVL growth, trading volume, liquidity depth, or other custom KPIs. Rewards scale based on performance, ensuring that incentive budgets are spent efficiently and only when objectives are being met.

This creates a win-win outcome: liquidity providers are rewarded for contributing to protocol growth, while protocols gain a more capital-efficient way to bootstrap and sustain their ecosystems. Any rewards that are not unlocked by achieving the target KPI can be returned to the campaign creator, reducing waste and improving ROI on incentive programs.

As DeFi matures, we believe incentive programs should be outcome-driven rather than emission-driven, and Metrom provides the infrastructure to make that possible.

About:

Squash is a **Pakistan-first DeFi neobank built on Sui**.

Pakistan already has strong crypto activity, but most users are still stuck between centralized exchanges, P2P markets, separate wallets, and disconnected DeFi platforms. There is no local consumer app that brings **on-ramp access, self-custody, swaps, DeFi discovery, payments, and crypto card infrastructure** into one simple mobile experience.

Squash is built to fill that gap.

The app gives Pakistani users a single path from crypto access to real on-chain usage: buying crypto, holding assets, sending payments, swapping tokens, discovering Sui DeFi opportunities, tracking supported positions, browsing ecosystem apps, and accessing future card-based payment flows.

The core pitch is simple: **Pakistan has crypto demand, but not a Sui-native DeFi neobank built around how local users actually enter, use, spend, and manage crypto.**

Squash turns that fragmented journey into one mobile product, with Sui as the settlement and infrastructure layer underneath.

The project also includes Move smart contracts for payment streams and card-linked infrastructure. The `card` module supports provider-linked cards, tiers, rewards accrual, reward claims, and deactivation, giving Squash an on-chain foundation for crypto card rewards and future spending experiences.

Squash aims to make Sui usable as everyday financial infrastructure in a large, underserved market where demand already exists.

About:

XORR — Buy Now, Pay Never · Private consumer credit on Sui

Today's BNPL is custodial, opaque, and credit-score-blind. XORR rebuilds it natively on Sui with three composable primitives behind one wallet:

🛒 Buy Now, Pay Never — Shoppers check out on unsecured credit (no collateral). In a single PTB the lending pool fronts the merchant and the buyer simply owes the loan — then DeepBook yield is routed in to repay it automatically. Pay later becomes pay never.

🔒 Confidential credit (the hard part, done for real) — Your income, debts, and history are Seal-encrypted (threshold IBE) in the browser. An AWS Nitro enclave is the only party that can decrypt them — enforced on-chain by a seal_approve policy that releases the key solely to the attestation-bound enclave. It scores you privately and posts a PCR-attested signature on-chain. Your raw financials never touch the chain or our servers. (Verified end-to-end on testnet: encrypted inputs → decrypted only via the on-chain policy → score 844 → applied.)

💧 Lend / Borrow + DeepBook yield — Supply USDC, DUSDC, or DEEP to earn real market-making yield; borrow over-collateralized or unsecured against your TEE credit line.

🏪 Merchant-ready — A drop-in @xorr/sdk lets any store accept "Pay with XORR" with one API key; settlement lands on-chain in a merchant escrow.

Built on Sui Move · Seal · Nautilus (AWS Nitro) · DeepBook. Five live apps, a published Move package, a confidential enclave, and an on-chain-verified BNPL + private-credit loop.

Live: [app.xorr.finance](http://app.xorr.finance) · [merchants.xorr.finance](http://merchants.xorr.finance) · [shop.xorr.finance](http://shop.xorr.finance) · [docs.xorr.finance](http://docs.xorr.finance) · [xorr.finance](http://xorr.finance)

About:

**Value Into Execution**

*Payments move value. Stelis makes that value executable.*

Stelis turns user-held value into execution capacity for programmable transactions on Sui.

This is not only a Sui problem. Across blockchains, users can hold value and still be unable to act because they do not hold the chain’s native gas asset.

Sui has already improved this experience for eligible stablecoin transfers. That is an important first step, but real products do not stop at transfers. A payment often needs to become an action: a purchase, subscription, game move, protocol call, or agent task.

Stelis addresses that next step: app actions that need Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTBs).

**A Concrete Flow**

A user holds USDC but no SUI.

The user approves an in-app purchase. The app builds its normal PTB. Stelis sponsors the transaction with SUI gas, then settles the execution cost and configured fee from the user’s USDC through a configured USDC-to-SUI settlement swap path.

The user does not need to acquire SUI first. The gas payer does not have to treat the transaction as a pure subsidy. The app does not need to replace its payment, fulfillment, or refund model.

This is the core distinction: Stelis is not trying to define how payment should work. It handles the execution-cost settlement layer that lets a payment become an executable Sui action.

**Execution and Settlement**

SUI remains the execution fuel.

The user’s held value becomes the settlement source for execution cost.

Stelis does not make Sui execution free, and it does not remove SUI from Sui execution. It reduces the need for users to buy, top up, and manage SUI before using an app.

In Stelis, a Host is the deployed execution environment that sponsors transactions and enforces settlement policy.

Stelis does not sponsor transactions blindly. In its regular settlement flow, the Host checks that the transaction fits configured settlement policy and has an eligible settlement swap path or reusable user-owned vault credit.

That turns sponsored execution from a subsidy model into a cost-recovering settlement flow.

**Built on Sui Primitives**

Stelis uses Sui sponsored transactions, but the product is the settlement model around them.

Stelis fits Sui because Sui already has the pieces this model needs:

- PTBs let an app action and its settlement step live in one transaction flow.

- Sponsored gas separates the transaction sender from the gas payer.

- Object ownership lets User Vault credit remain controlled by the user.

- Sui’s object model lets apps keep their own objects and business logic. Stelis does not force apps to store product state inside Stelis.

These primitives let Stelis add settlement around execution without taking over the app’s state or business logic.

**Trust and Ownership**

Users still approve and sign the transaction.

A Host can sponsor execution, but it does not get to treat user assets as its own liquidity. User Vault credit is a user-owned settlement source, and withdrawal authority stays with the user through the vault module.

The regular settlement flow is not an open-ended gas subsidy. The Host sponsors a transaction after checking settlement policy, the configured settlement swap path, and any available user vault credit.

**Open Composition**

Stelis is not a checkout format, payment protocol, merchant app, or refund standard.

Apps keep their own PTB structure, object model, payment flow, fulfillment logic, and refund policy. Existing payment SDKs, merchant backends, external payment protocols, and future standards can compose with Stelis instead of being replaced by it.

Developers can connect to an existing Host, or operate their own when they need control over supported tokens, settlement swap paths, fees, liquidity, and risk policy.

**Product Tokens Become Execution Assets**

Stelis is not limited to stablecoins.

Stablecoins are a natural starting point, but the model also matters for tokens issued by apps, games, protocols, and communities.

Settlement still needs a path into SUI. Stelis uses DeepBook, Sui’s on-chain liquidity infrastructure, for configured SUI settlement swap paths. Tokens are not accepted automatically; each settlement swap path must pass pricing, fee, liquidity, and request admission checks.

For token issuers, this creates a practical reason to maintain healthy SUI liquidity. Better liquidity does not only help trading. It can make the token more useful inside the product itself.

A game token can fund game actions. A protocol token can fund protocol actions. A community token can become usable inside services built around that community.

In this model, liquidity becomes usability infrastructure, and token utility becomes tied to actual execution.

**What Is Built**

The repository includes the Move settlement package, TypeScript SDK, MCP server for agent clients, deployable Host API, public web app, and admin app.

The implementation includes on-chain settlement, User Vault ownership, Host-side preparation and sponsorship, settlement swap path admission checks, SDK integration, agent-facing MCP tools, and operator surfaces.

Stelis is not only a description of a UX problem. It is an implementation of a settlement model for programmable Sui applications.

**Early Testnet Benchmark**

An early Sui testnet benchmark is included to make the settlement overhead measurable.
The benchmark compares direct SUI-paid empty transactions with Host-sponsored empty transactions that use existing User Vault credit. In the current test set, direct empty SUI transactions averaged 0.001009880 SUI over 150 runs, while sponsored User Vault credit-use transactions averaged 0.002012092 SUI over 117 runs.

The repeated sponsored path added about 0.001002212 SUI of gas over the direct empty-transaction baseline in this test set. Under the tested configuration, the Host also recovered the paid gas plus a configured 0.000100000 SUI margin per run.

This should be read as a testnet measurement of settlement overhead for this empty transaction shape, not as a mainnet claim, a price quote, or a proportional gas multiplier. User Vault creation is measured separately as setup cost.

Test account: [0x57ec...fd99](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/account/0x57ec4274b7f95e180a9533db1f4ffb48b2751281b575fde9d0014bc5a3fcfd99/portfolio)

Report: [Execution Benchmark](https://github.com/stelis-dev/stelis/blob/main/docs/onchain-tests/reports/empty-execution-benchmark.md)

**Closing**

Held value should not sit idle because execution requires a different asset. Stelis turns it into execution capacity.

###

**World AI CORE** is a next-generation digital identity platform built around the concept of a unified digital entity known as a Core.

Today, people manage dozens of accounts, usernames, wallet addresses, social profiles, and digital identities across different platforms. World AI CORE introduces a new approach by bringing all of these fragmented elements together into a single digital entity capable of existing and interacting across multiple digital environments.

The platform is designed around four stages of evolution:

**Core Identity → Signature Protocol → Social Graph → Creator Economy**

The first stage establishes a unique Core for every user, including a **Core ID**, profile, and reputation layer.

The second stage introduces the Signature Protocol. Each Core automatically generates a visual and audio signature. The visual signature is based on a unique 36×36 machine-readable structure designed to be recognized by both people and devices.

The third stage expands the Core into a social layer where users build relationships, join communities, and develop digital reputation through meaningful interactions.

The fourth stage unlocks the Creator Economy, enabling creators, communities, and projects to exchange value through ecosystem energy, reputation systems, rewards, achievements, and Core Keys.

The entire platform operates through a simple interaction cycle:

**Create Core → Generate Signature → Connect → Support → Grow**

Users create a Core, receive a unique Signature, connect with other participants, support projects and communities, and build reputation across the ecosystem.

What makes World AI CORE different from traditional social platforms and digital wallets is its unified identity model.

**A single Core automatically generates:**

**• Digital Identity
• Visual Signature
• Audio Signature
• Social Profile
• Reputation System**

All derived from one origin.

**World AI CORE** is currently being developed as an MVP within a broader digital identity research initiative.

**The roadmap includes:**

• Core Signature Protocol
• Audio Signatures
• Core Passport
• Communities & Social Graph
• Creator Economy
• Digital Worlds Integration

World AI CORE is not another social network.

It is not another wallet.

It is a new digital identity layer where people, communities, creators, and intelligent systems can interact through a single Core that exists across multiple digital worlds.

**Vision**

One Core.

One Identity.

Infinite Possibilities.

**
World AI CORE**

Your Identity.

Your Signature.

Your Universe.

About:

### **Gally: The Real-World Asset Capitalization Protocol**

A trustless, high-performance protocol on Sui that transforms real-world economic output into a programmable DeFi primitive. Gally enables global liquidity to form around vetted, physical assets; from heavy infrastructure and trade finance to energy and real estate; issuing yield-bearing digital deeds (`GallyShares`) powered by $O(1)$ lazy-index distribution, milestone-gated capital enforcement, and slashable validator accountability.

### **The Paradigm Shift (Why it Exists)**

Historically, institutional-grade private credit and RWA yield have been gatekept by intermediaries, requiring investors to blindly trust centralized operators with their capital. Gally strips away the intermediaries and replaces trust with mathematics. By collateralizing every human action and escrowing every dollar, Gally structurally eliminates counterparty risk, allowing global retail capital to fund the physical world and capture its yield safely.

### **The Protocol Architecture**

- **Deterministic Capital Formation:** Global capital pools as USDC in an on-chain smart escrow. Funding is strictly all-or-nothing. If a raise hits its target, capital converts to yield-bearing entity tokens (`<T>`). If it falls short, capital is fully refunded. Funds are never stranded.

- **Tranche-Gated Capital Deployment:** Project operators do not receive upfront blank checks. Capital is locked in protocol escrow and released strictly via predefined tranches. Every release is gated by a hard deadline and a cryptographically signed proof of real-world progress. Miss a deadline, and the protocol automatically routes to a default and compensation path.

- **Slashable Economic Accountability:** Legal and physical attestations are decentralized. Protocol validators must lock USDC to vouch for an asset's legal documentation and milestone proofs. If a validator approves a fraudulent or failing milestone, they are slashed. Any user can post a bond to dispute a vouch, triggering a decentralized jury resolution that mathematically guarantees restitution for investors.

- **Unconditional Self-Custody:** Every investor action is fundamentally permissionless. The protocol is designed with absolute exit guarantees. Even in the event of an emergency protocol pause, exit cranks—refunds, yield claims, unwrapping, and redemptions—remain permanently open. The protocol can never trap your funds.

***The Trust Thesis:** The entity is never trusted. The validator is trusted only up to the exact value of their locked collateral. The protocol admin is trusted only with system parameters. **No one is ever trusted with your money.**
