About:

**Suize is the payment rail for AI agents on Sui — Stripe for the agent economy.**

Any AI agent can pay any merchant, gasless, in a single transaction — and any merchant can accept it with one line of code. Payments today are built for humans; agents can't use them. Suize is the rail they can.

It runs on **x402**, the open agent-payment standard — we wrote the Sui implementation (open PRs upstream). Our facilitator is keyless and stateless: it settles on-chain but never holds your funds or keys — so the on-chain receipt is the proof.

On top sits a consumer **AI wallet**: sign in with Google, fully non-custodial, then give your agent a capped sub-account — a 1-of-2 multisig it can *never* overspend, enforced on-chain — with a private, verifiable log of everything it does, anchored on-chain. Merchants get paid with **@suize/pay** (live on npm) or a no-code charge link; subscriptions are on-chain objects you cancel by deleting.

We run our whole company on this rail — an AI web host, a prediction market, and a live directory of agent commerce. Live on testnet, mainnet-ready.

About:

**Bringing Japan's local fervor to the global prediction market.**

**We are building a prediction market on the Sui blockchain dedicated to unique Japanese sports and culture—such as Sumo, Koshien (high school baseball), NPB (Nippon Professional Baseball), and the J.League. Despite boasting incredibly passionate fanbases, these vibrant, niche markets remain vastly underserved by existing global platforms.

### 2. Detailed Description

Current prediction market platforms tend to skew heavily toward Western-centric macroeconomics, US elections, or mainstream global soccer. However, the true essence of sports and cultural enthusiasm often lies in deeply rooted "local contexts."

This project is a next-generation prediction market targeting Japan's uniquely passionate sports culture—from the daily win-loss records of Sumo wrestlers and the intense, single-elimination drama of Koshien, to the NPB pennant race and the fierce promotion/relegation battles of the J.League.

Our platform is not merely a betting site; it is a decentralized engagement hub where fans can prove their "knowledge" and "love" for the game while sharing their passion with the community.

By leveraging the **Sui blockchain**—renowned for its ultra-fast transaction processing, low gas fees, and object-centric data model—we can achieve real-time odds fluctuations synchronized with live match progress and deliver an incredibly smooth UI/UX. This ensures a frictionless, stress-free experience even for general sports fans who are completely unfamiliar with Web3.

### 3. Team & Vision

Our team thrives on a diverse, highly effective cross-border synergy between Japan and India.

- **Cultural Insight & Product Design (Japan Team):**

Members with a deep understanding of Japanese sports, cultural nuances, fan psychology, and the core sources of local enthusiasm lead our unique market design and creative direction.

- **Technical Excellence & Execution (India Team):**

**Highly talented student developers from India** serve as the core technical engine of our project. Bringing immense passion and flexibility to the development process, they are exceptionally fast at adapting to cutting-edge Web3 technologies. They are the driving force behind our scalable backend architecture and the robust implementation of Sui smart contracts (Move).

By fusing "authentic Japanese local insights" with the "top-tier technical execution of Indian student developers," we are building a world-class product on the Sui ecosystem that goes far beyond a simple local application.

### 4. Why Sui?

- **Real-Time Execution & Low Cost:**

Building and executing prediction markets in milliseconds as sports matches rapidly unfold requires the unmatched throughput and sub-second latency that only Sui can provide.

- **The Path to Mass Adoption (zkLogin):**

To bring traditional sports fans onboard without the intimidating friction of managing seed phrases, Sui’s native zkLogin enables users to seamlessly log in and participate using their everyday social media accounts. This is a game-changer for mainstream user acquisition.

- **Uncompromised Security with Move:**

Given the high liquidity and financial nature of prediction markets, the programmable asset security offered by the Move language is absolutely vital. It provides a highly secure, intuitive, and reliable development environment for our engineering crew.

About:

TrustGate is a behavioral trust scoring primitive for Sui. It reads onchain

activity (wallet age, transaction history, object deployments, interaction

patterns, bot signals) and turns it into a portable trust score any protocol

can gate on.

The Sui-native part: instead of a payment gate or an oracle call at integration

time, wallets that pass the threshold earn a non-transferable TrustedAgentCap,

a Move capability object. Protocols just check whether a wallet holds the cap.

No oracle call on their end. The cap can't be sold, lent, or transferred,

because it's minted without the store ability. Two tiers, standard (70+) and

elite (85+), each with epoch-based expiry so trust stays fresh.

What's live on testnet:

- ScoreRegistry storing scores per address, written only by the authorized oracle

- TrustedAgentCap and TrustedAgentCapElite minting flow

- A DeepBook-style gated pool that requires a cap to place an order, and rejects

wallets that don't hold one

- TokenShield, which scores a token by the trust composition of its holders and

traders, not just the contract. High volume from low-scored wallets drags the

token score down. Sui's object model makes this clean to compute.

Live at [sui.trustgated.xyz](http://sui.trustgated.xyz). Wallet scoring, token scoring, and the gated pool

demo are all on the site.

About:

**SONARI**

A transparent donation platform for verified aid on Sui.

----------------------------------------------------------

**THE PROBLEM**

After a disaster, physical supplies reach affected areas — but direct

financial support to individual survivors is far harder to deliver at

scale. The hard part isn't only moving money. A program must decide who

is genuinely affected, who is a real person, who registered before the

event, who has already claimed, and how to follow different rules across

countries.

That complexity is why cash aid usually depends on slow manual review,

local intermediaries, or country-specific systems. It's difficult for

people anywhere in the world to fund a transparent campaign and trust

that money will actually reach verified individuals.

The stakes are large. Global charitable giving runs into the hundreds of

billions of dollars annually (U.S. giving alone exceeded $550B in recent

years), yet donors routinely have little visibility into where funds sit

or why a given person qualifies for support.

----------------------------------------------------------

**WHAT SONARI DOES**

Sonari makes both the money flow AND the eligibility decision verifiable.

• Donors can see where funds are held — on-chain.

• Recipients can see why they qualify — programmatically.

• Sui Move contracts enforce the final rules.

• Nautilus-backed verifiers turn real-world facts into signed

results the contracts can check inside a TEE.

Sonari is NOT insurance. Donations don't guarantee payouts. Relief

depends on pool balances, program rules, verification, fraud controls,

and claim timing.

----------------------------------------------------------

**HOW IT WORKS (earthquake MVP)**

1. Official earthquake data (USGS / ShakeMap) is re-fetched and

verified inside a Nautilus TEE, which signs a finalized payload.

2. A verified disaster automatically creates an on-chain relief

campaign.

3. A recipient proves membership, residence, and identity

(World ID is the live identity route).

4. Sui Move checks the signed disaster result, identity, the

affected-area proof, duplicate-claim state, and pool balances

before paying relief in USDC.

Payouts arrive in two stages: an immediate floor payment, then a later

pro-rata campaign payout. Crucially, payout amounts are fixed by signed

data — NOT first-come-first-served — so claiming earlier never lets one

recipient drain the pool ahead of others.

----------------------------------------------------------

**WHY IT'S MORE THAN EARTHQUAKES**

Earthquakes are the first use case, not the limit of the design. The

same pattern — re-fetch an official source inside a TEE, sign the result,

let Sui accept only signed results — extends to ANY verifiable event:

• Other disasters: floods, typhoons, tsunami, wildfire, evacuation

orders, once a source policy is defined.

• Community support: student IDs, university accounts, enrollment

checks → scholarships, tuition aid, emergency grants.

No raw personal data is stored on-chain — only verification state,

hashes, and claim records.

About:

**Bean is the payment lane for autonomous commerce on Sui: non-custodial receiver objects, Circle CCTP settlement, permissionless sweeping, and agent-ready .**

**What Is Bean Protocol?**

Bean is the payment lane for autonomous commerce on Sui.

It gives merchants, apps, and agents non-custodial payment lanes that can receive stablecoin payments from multiple chains and settle through explicit onchain rules.

Each lane is a receiver: a chain-native settlement object/account/contract that owns the payment context, not a Bean-controlled custody wallet. On Sui, receivers are shared objects and funds are held as `Coin<T>` / `Balance<T>` values, making the custody boundary visible and enforceable.

Bean uses Circle CCTP for cross-chain USDC settlement, so payments move through burn-and-mint instead of wrapped assets or pooled custody. Once a lane is funded, settlement is permissionless: owners can sweep directly, and independent sentinels can move payments forward for a bounded reward.

This makes Bean especially useful for autonomous agents. An agent can request a payment lane, receive or route funds, and trigger settlement without ever becoming the custodian of merchant treasury.

Bean has already processed over $20K in real transaction volume in its first month live. Now, on Sui, Bean turns checkout into programmable payment infrastructure for autonomous commerce.

`AI Agent Wallet is an **on-chain custody wallet for AI agents**, built natively on
Sui. An owner opens a dedicated wallet for their AI assistant and sets the spending
rules **once, on-chain** — per-transaction, daily, weekly and monthly limits,
recipient allow-/block-lists, cooldowns and rate limits. The agent then spends
**autonomously with its own key**, and every rule is enforced by a Move smart
contract rather than a backend. The owner can inspect, freeze, or claw back funds at
any time.

In one line: **a spending drawer with guardrails for AI agents** — the agent can pay
on its own, but only inside the limits the owner defined on-chain.`

About:

**SuiPredict** is a Sui-native decentralized prediction market. The entire trading and settlement logic lives on-chain in Move: positions are priced by an **LMSR automated market maker**, and markets resolve through an **optimistic oracle** with a dispute window rather than a centralized adjudicator. For crypto-price markets, a live price feed proposes the outcome and a challenge period lets anyone dispute it before it's finalized.

Beyond standard binary markets, SuiPredict supports **multi-outcome** and **scalar** (numeric-range) markets, **parlays** (combining predictions for higher payouts), short-duration **micro-markets**, and **copy-trading / leaderboards**. Built on Sui, it inherits parallel execution for sub-second confirmation, very low gas, and an object model where each position is an asset the user truly owns.

**Stack:** Move 2024 smart contracts (testnet package `0xcc20…55fd`), a React + TypeScript + Vite dApp using `@mysten/dapp-kit`, and decentralized hosting on Walrus Sites. The app is internationalized (English, Traditional Chinese, Japanese).

**Live demo:** [https://suipredict.vercel.app](https://suipredict.vercel.app)

About:

**Reiy Finance — Intent-based Meta DEX Aggregator on Sui**

Reiy lets you describe the result you want instead of picking pools, routes, and slippage yourself — a network of solvers then competes to deliver the best execution.

**The problem** Trading on Sui still puts all the work on the user. You pick the route and set the slippage before the market ever competes for your order. Opposing orders never meet, doubling price impact, and a weak minimum leaves value on the table.

**How it works**

- You submit an intent on-chain — your tokens escrow inside your own Intent object, with a protected minimum.

- The Execution Coordinator (Fisherman) collects intents into a batch and runs a competitive auction off-chain.

- Solvers compete, finding direct coincidence-of-wants matches or routing through Sui liquidity such as DeepBook and Cetus.

- The Coordinator signs the winning solution; the Move contract verifies the signature, enforces your floor, and settles atomically on-chain.

**Key mechanisms**

- **Hot-Potato Settlement** — settlement returns a receipt that cannot be stored or dropped, forcing every trade to complete in one atomic transaction.

- **Escrow Protection** — funds stay locked in your own intent, and the contract rejects any payout below the minimum you agreed to.

- **SBBO Floor** — at submit time the protocol sets a slippage-bounded floor from the on-chain price, so whoever wins, your fill never lands below it.

- **Signed Certificates** — the Coordinator signs the winner off-chain, but the chain verifies the signature and your minimum before any funds move. You trust it for speed, never for safety.

**Why Sui** Sub-second finality, low fees, and the object model let us keep funds escrowed and settle the auction atomically on-chain — the part that is prohibitively expensive on EVM. The coincidence-of-wants model is proven at scale on Ethereum, yet no CoW-style protocol exists on Sui today.

**What's built** Live on Sui testnet: Move contracts for intent escrow and certificate-based settlement, the Fisherman coordinator running the full auction loop, and two integrated solvers — an on-chain aggregator that water-fills across DeepBook and Cetus, and an inventory market maker.

**Published Resources (testnet)**

- **API **— [https://testnet-fisherman.reiy.finance](https://testnet-fisherman.reiy.finance)

- **API Docs** — [https://testnet-fisherman.reiy.finance/docs](https://testnet-fisherman.reiy.finance/docs)

About:

**VeryTontine: Community DeFi Protocol for the Unbanked**

We're bringing programmable DeFi to 1 billion unbanked people in Africa and beyond.

**The Problem:**

In Sub-Saharan Africa, over 1 billion people rely on informal savings circles called "Tontines" or "Ikirimbas"—managing over $1 trillion annually using metal boxes and paper notebooks. When records are lost or tampered with, entire communities lose their savings. There's no transparency, no credit history, and no access to modern financial tools.

**Our Solution:**

VeryTontine is a community DeFi protocol that transforms informal savings circles into programmable smart contract vaults with automated lending, trust-based credit scoring, and transparent governance.

**Core DeFi Features:**

• **Programmable Vaults**: Smart contract treasuries with automated contribution tracking and payout distribution

• **Trust-Based Lending**: Members can borrow from the community pool at 10% interest, with on-chain credit scoring determining eligibility

• **Dual Payout Models**: Traditional round-robin tontines OR equal profit-sharing community banking

• **Yield Distribution**: Interest from loans is automatically distributed to all members

• **On-Chain Reputation**: Every contribution, loan repayment, and default is recorded, creating a verifiable financial identity

Technical Implementation on Sui:

• **Move Smart Contracts** with O(1) scalability optimizations (using Tables instead of vectors to prevent gas bombs)

• **Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTBs)** for atomic circle + vault creation in a single transaction

• **zkLogin Integration**: Users onboard with Google OAuth—no seed phrases, no crypto knowledge required

• **Shinami Gas Sponsorship**: Zero-gas transactions make DeFi accessible to users who can't afford SUI

• **Offline-First Architecture**: Isar local database with background sync ensures the app works in low-connectivity environments

**Walrus Integration:**

We use Walrus as a decentralized reputation layer. Each savings circle's charter (rules and agreements) is stored on Walrus with the blob ID anchored directly in the on-chain Circle object. Our AI-generated financial health reports are also stored on Walrus, creating a tamper-proof audit trail that follows users across circles—enabling the unbanked to build verifiable credit histories for the first time.

**AI-Native Features:**

Our autonomous AI Treasurer (powered by Groq Llama 3) analyzes on-chain transaction history to assess default risk, suggest payout timing, and generate Trust Badges for reliable members. All AI reports are stored on Walrus for verifiable decision-making.

**Real-World Impact:**

Our team is from Burundi, East Africa—we're building for our own community first. We understand the cultural context of Ikirimbas and the trust dynamics that make them work. This isn't a hypothetical use case; it's infrastructure for people we know.

**Current Status:**

• ✅ Deployed on Sui Testnet (Package ID: 0x0237619f02ff683a26c5f0885c57f88583728de89e47b0d85d4a063dd38e8ba2)

• ✅ Production Flutter app with offline support

• ✅ Admin dashboard for ecosystem monitoring

• ✅ Comprehensive Move test suite

• 🔜 Mainnet deployment Q3 2026

**Why This is DeFi:**

We're not building a "savings app." We're building a community finance protocol with lending, yield generation, risk management, and programmable treasuries—core DeFi primitives reimagined for real-world adoption. Instead of asking "How do we bank the unbanked?" we ask "How do we give them programmable money they can trust?"

About:

x402-sui-stack — the x402 builder stack for Sui.

Two things no other Sui x402 project can claim: a real payment settled on Sui mainnet that you can recompute yourself in-browser, and a co-authored extension to the x402 spec itself (#2666).

x402 is the open, HTTP-native payment standard for the agent economy: a server answers "402 Payment Required" with machine-readable terms, a client signs an exact payment, and a facilitator settles it on-chain. x402-sui-stack is everything a developer needs to use x402 on Sui — a live settlement rail, the dev tooling around it, the standards work, and a runnable demo that pays a real API call and verifies it on-chain.

The problem
AI agents are starting to pay for things — APIs, data, compute — at fractions of a cent. That only works on a chain with sub-cent fees and sub-second finality: Sui. But there was no x402 settlement rail on Sui, and no resource-side example showing a builder how to gate their own Sui API behind a payment. This stack is that missing layer.

What's in it
• Rail — the first x402 facilitator to settle on Sui mainnet. Non-custodial, zero-fee, with an optional Enoki gas station so payers need no SUI for gas.
github.com/DrVelvetFog/sui-x402-facilitator (live: [sui-facilitator.onrender.com](http://sui-facilitator.onrender.com))
• Tooling — a Claude Code plugin + an MCP server that lints x402 flows and recomputes any settlement from on-chain truth.
github.com/DrVelvetFog/x402-pilot
• Standard — a co-authored settlement-receipt binding extension to the x402 spec.
github.com/x402-foundation/x402/pull/2666
• Demo (this repo) — a self-contained buyer + an x402-gated seller (the resource-side template the rail repo doesn't ship) that settles a paid call on Sui and recomputes it.
github.com/DrVelvetFog/x402-sui-stack

Live on mainnet — verify it yourself
A real $0.01 USDC payment settled on Sui mainnet (tx EVGJpzy383YkrZntGgTm1SZBeXPqguKG9euypYptsKrm); the on-chain net credit to the recipient equals the advertised amount. Open the live site, paste the digest, and recompute it in your browser against a public Sui fullnode — no trust required.
Live demo: [x402-sui-stack.netlify.app](http://x402-sui-stack.netlify.app)

Why Sui
Sub-cent fees + sub-second finality (the only economics under which per-call agent payments work), sponsored transactions via Enoki (agents pay in USDC without holding SUI), and non-custodial by construction — the facilitator can only simulate or broadcast the payer's own signed transaction; it holds no keys and never touches funds.

Built for Sui Overflow 2026 — DeFi & Payments (Infrastructure & Tooling). Apache-2.0.

India's CCTS market creates $6B in regulated demand. Zero digital infrastructure exists for it. We are building it — natively on Sui.

About:

`**Splyt — fixed, predictable yield for everyone, on Sui.**

In DeFi, principal and yield are welded together, so every "APY" is a *floating* rate that can collapse the day after you deposit. There is no on-chain way to simply lock a fixed return.

I built Splyt because of my uncle. He saved for three years in Nigeria toward a home for his family abroad, and asked me to help him into crypto. I put his money into something paying almost 18%. The rate was floating — it quietly fell to almost nothing. He didn't get angry; he just asked if his money was still safe. He wasn't greedy. He wanted **certainty**. That didn't exist on-chain.

Splyt is **yield tokenization (Pendle-style), built Sui-native.** One deposit of a yield-bearing asset splits into two tradeable Sui coins:

- **PT (Principal Token):** redeems 1:1 at maturity — buy it below par to **lock a fixed yield**. For savers who want certainty.
- **YT (Yield Token):** a claim on all the yield the deposit earns — **leveraged yield exposure**. For traders.

Certainty for one person and upside for another — out of the same deposit.

**Why it matters:** fixed income is the largest asset class on Earth (~$140T) and barely exists on-chain. Pendle proved the demand on Ethereum (~$8.7B TVL, ~$70B in yield settled) — but Sui had no yield-tokenization primitive at all. Splyt fills that gap and showcases what only the Sui stack can do cleanly:

- **DeepBook v3:** PT and YT are standard Sui coins specifically so they list on Sui's native order book. We built the full trade lifecycle — per-user BalanceManager, deposit + place limit order in one transaction, live order book, cancel, and withdraw — proven live on-chain.
- **Walrus:** the yield-curve history is stored content-addressed on Walrus, and the blob id is **committed on-chain**. The frontend reads that id from the Market and fetches the raw series — a yield history anyone can verify, not a number our server made up.
- **OpenZeppelin (Contracts for Sui):** overflow-safe `mul_div` (round-down) for exact pro-rata YT payouts that can never exceed the pool.

**Live and verified on Sui testnet:** split / combine / redeem, the full DeepBook trade path, and the Walrus-anchored yield chart — all working, with Move and script test suites passing. Try it: **splyttapp.vercel.app**
`

About:

**BROWNFI propAMM**

BrownFi invents a novel pricing mechanism for spot AMM based on oracles, to offer high capital efficiency, flexible market making, and simple UX. The core concept of BrowmFi AMM employs an elastic parameterization of a Limit Orderbook (LOB).

Instead of operating like a traditional AMM (like Uniswap) that derives prices from bonding curves or pool depth, BrownFi acts more like an RFQ/PopAMM with oracle-based prices at a competitive rate but requiring very little capital. This brings BrownFi some advantages over other AMMs:

- High capital efficiency with effortless position management

- No out-of-range risk

- Dynamic market making from simple to complex strategies by tuning a set of parameters.

BrownFi is built on the same mathematical codebase as Uniswap V2, but extends it with oracle integration, skewness-based incentives, and - in V3 - asymmetric liquidity depth and bid-ask spread.

### **Advantages**

The core promise: deposit, earn, withdraw. No active management. No going out of range. No structural bleed to arbitrageurs.

### **Your liquidity is always working, no out-of-range dead zones**

In Uniswap V3, LPs choose a price range. When the market moves outside it, their liquidity stops earning fees entirely while still holding a losing position. BrownFi requires no range selection at all. Kappa concentrates liquidity around the current oracle price automatically — every dollar is always deployed and earning, regardless of where the market moves.

### **You earn more per dollar than full-range LPs**

Because BrownFi concentrates liquidity around the oracle price, each dollar of capital provides significantly more depth than an equivalent deposit in a full-range pool. More depth means capturing a larger share of swap fees on the same capital. At K=0.1, a BrownFi LP earns roughly 18× the fee income of a Uniswap V2 full-range LP with the same deposit.

### **You are structurally protected against LVR**

Loss-Versus-Rebalancing (LVR) is the single largest hidden cost for LPs in traditional AMMs. It happens because the pool's price lags the global market, arbitrageurs exploit this gap every time the price moves, extracting value directly from LP pockets. BrownFi eliminates this by pricing every swap from a live oracle. The pool always trades at or near the global market price, leaving nothing for arbitrageurs to extract.

### **Your losses are capped when the pool gets imbalanced**

Even with the best oracle, markets can move fast and leave a pool temporarily imbalanced. BrownFi V3 enforces a hard limit: no single trade can push the pool beyond a gamma threshold  ( 90%/10% by default at the beginning). Beyond that threshold, the pool stops accepting further one-sided swaps. This directly limits the worst-case inventory exposure an LP can face from a single directional move.

### **You earn higher fees precisely when the market is most dangerous**

When market conditions become turbulent, price feeds diverging, volatility spiking, most AMM LPs earn the same flat fee while bearing much higher risk. BrownFi V3's dynamic spread automatically widens during these periods, charging traders more to access your liquidity when it is most at risk. Volatility becomes a source of income rather than pure cost.

### **You get the best of both Uniswap V2 and V3 — without their downsides**

The fairest benchmark for a BrownFi LP is not buy-and-hold but a constant-mix portfolio — the strategy Uniswap V2 implicitly follows by keeping reserves in a 50/50 dollar-value balance. Against this baseline, BrownFi V3 wins on both dimensions that define LP returns:

- Lower IL than Uniswap V2: Because BrownFi prices every swap from a live oracle, it eliminates LVR (the structural loss that bleeds Uniswap V2 LPs every time the market moves and arbitrageurs close the gap). BrownFi LPs hold a more stable portfolio value under the same market conditions.

- Higher yield than Uniswap V2: Liquidity concentration via Kappa means BrownFi captures significantly more trading fees per dollar deployed, comparable to a narrow-range Uniswap V3 position, but without the management overhead or out-of-range risk.

The result is consistent outperformance: in the long run, a BrownFi LP position compounds faster than a passive constant-mix portfolio and, over time, surpasses a simple buy-and-hold strategy as accumulated fees and reduced IL compound together.

### **Your fees compound automatically, no active management required**

In Uniswap V3 (CLMM), fees accumulate outside the LP position and must be manually collected and reinvested. Every time you forget to harvest, you are leaving compounding returns on the table, and each reinvestment costs gas. BrownFi, like Uniswap V2, automatically reinvests all trading fees back into the pool's reserves. Your LP token grows in value with every swap, with zero action required on your part. For passive LPs, this auto-compounding effect alone meaningfully improves long-run returns compared to CLMM alternatives.

### **Capital Efficiency**

Unlike Uniswap V3 where liquidity can go out-of-range, BrownFi's oracle-anchored concentration is always active and always deployed around the current market price. Performance analysis showed BrownFi V2 already outperforms Uniswap V3 at equivalent capital concentration on average (higher net PnL vs buy-and-hold). BrownFi V3 further improves this, especially in bear markets.

Read more [https://brownfi.gitbook.io/brownfi-docs](https://brownfi.gitbook.io/brownfi-docs)

About:

`# Morita — Cross-Game Inventory & Commerce Protocol on Sui

**Sui Overflow 2026 | DeFi & Payments Track**

**Package ID:** `0x68145a7f8c14ba84c250b81277b4e0060be65ca13242052d26a18e5e0cc977b3`

---

## The Problem

Game items are trapped inside walled gardens. A legendary sword earned in one game cannot be sold, traded, or used anywhere else. Players accumulate hundreds of hours of value with zero liquidity.

Developers who want to tokenize their in-game assets face a steep wall: write and audit custom smart contracts, manage cryptographic keys, fund gas for every mint, build a marketplace from scratch, and figure out how to make royalties actually enforceable. Most give up before they start.

---

## What Morita Does

Morita is a full-stack, plug-and-play protocol that connects game economies on Sui. Developers register their game through a web dashboard, define item templates, and publish with one click. A REST API lets game servers mint items and generate one-click claim URLs for players.

Players sign in with Google (no wallet extension, no seed phrase, no gas fees), see all their items across every game in a unified inventory, and trade freely — sell for SUI with automatic royalties, or barter atomically across games.

---

## What Makes Morita Different

### 1. Zero Blockchain Knowledge Required for Developers

Most Web3 gaming platforms require developers to write Solidity or Move smart contracts. Morita handles everything on-chain behind the scenes. Developers use a familiar web dashboard + REST API — exactly like any other B2B SaaS. The Sui Move contracts, PTB construction, Kiosk integration, and gas sponsorship are all abstracted away.

- Dashboard: log in with Google, create a publisher workspace, register games, define item templates
- API: simple REST calls with Bearer token auth — `POST /api/v1/game/:id/item/mint` returns a claim URL
- No Solidity, no Rust, no private key management. Just HTTP.

### 2. Gas-Free for Everyone, Always

Every transaction on Morita is sponsored by the platform via Enoki gas stations. Developers never spend SUI minting items. Players never spend SUI trading.

No token deposits. No gas calculations. No "you need 0.01 SUI to perform this action" errors. This is critical for mainstream gaming adoption — asking players to manage gas kills retention before the first trade.

### 3. Atomic Cross-Game Barter

Players can lock an item from Game A into escrow, set conditions (rarity, item type, specific game), and another player from Game B can fulfill the barter in a single Sui Programmable Transaction Block.

Both items transfer or neither does. If the values don't match, the gap is covered with SUI atomically (`fulfill_escrow_with_value`). This is not a marketplace listing — it is trust-minimized P2P swapping across entirely separate game economies.

### 4. Protocol-Level Royalty Enforcement

Most NFT marketplaces enforce royalties through contract logic that can be — and routinely is — bypassed by using custom contracts or trading off-platform.

Morita uses native Sui Kiosk transfer policies. The royalty rules live at the Sui protocol layer, not in application code. When an item is purchased through `kiosk_ext::buy_item`, the `TransferPolicy<GameItem>` confirms the transfer and the royalty split executes atomically in the same transaction. There is no way to circumvent it.

### 5. On-Chain Items, Off-Chain Metadata

Every item is a first-class Sui object (`GameItem`) with guaranteed safety through the Move type system. Each object carries its game ID, item type, rarity, and a blob ID referencing its metadata.

Large assets — images, 3D model data, JSON specifications — are stored on Walrus, a decentralized blob storage network built by Mysten Labs. The blockchain stores only a lightweight blob ID reference, keeping gas costs minimal while ensuring metadata is immutable and permanently accessible.

### 6. Complete Architecture, Not a Demo

| Layer | Technology | What It Does |
|-------|-----------|--------------|
| **Smart Contracts** | 4 Sui Move modules | Registry, item, kiosk, escrow |
| **Frontend** | Next.js 16, 18 pages | Dashboard, inventory, marketplace, claim, barter |
| **Backend** | 29 server actions, Hono REST API | Business logic, PTB builders, Enoki client |
| **Auth** | Enoki zkLogin (Google OAuth) | Zero-wallet sign-in, sponsored transactions |
| **Database** | PostgreSQL + Drizzle ORM | 11 tables: games, items, claims, escrows, kiosks |
| **Storage** | Walrus | Decentralized item metadata and images |
| **Transactions** | 11 PTB builders + 2 signing flows | All user tx sponsored, admin tx executed server-side |

**4 Smart Contracts:**
- `registry.move` — Publisher registration, game lifecycle (draft → published), admin controls (pause, resume, verify). Uses OTW pattern for one-time initialization.
- `item.move` — GameItem type with mint/burn. Double-mint prevention via minted_items Table inside Game object.
- `kiosk_ext.move` — Kiosk marketplace wrapper. List items for sale, buy with automatic transfer policy confirmation, deploy transfer policies.
- `escrow.move` — Atomic barter engine. Lock items with conditions (`itemIdTarget`, `gameIdAccept`, `itemTypeAccept`, `rarityAccept`), fulfill with optional SUI value gap coverage, or cancel to return.

**Two Signing Flows:**
- **Flow A (executeAsAdmin):** Backend builds PTB → Enoki sponsors → admin (platform) signs → execute. Used for mint, claim redemption, admin operations.
- **Flow B (sponsorForUser + executeUserSigned):** Frontend builds PTB → Enoki sponsors → user signs via Enoki keypair → execute. Used for create publisher, publish game, list for sale, buy, all escrow operations.

All Enoki API calls (ZKP generation, nonce creation, JWT resolution) are proxied through server actions using the private key — no client-side EnokiFlow dependency, zero configuration issues.

**11 PTB Builders:** mint, burn, createPublisher, publish (2-step initiate + finalize), listForSale, buyItem, lockForAny, lockForTarget, fulfill, fulfillWithValue, cancel, createKiosk.

### 7. Built for Real-World Testing

- **18 frontend pages** — Developer dashboard (overview, games, items, API keys, analytics, activity) + Player hub (inventory, item detail, marketplace, barter, claim, history)
- **29 server actions** — Thin wrappers over 6 business logic services
- **11 database tables** — gamedevs, publishers, games, item_templates, claim_codes, api_keys, user_kiosks, kiosk_listings, escrow_index, item_cache, tx_events
- **15 automated tests** — Covering all PTB builders, Enoki client exports, and marketplace services
- **0 `any` types, 0 `useEffect` cascades** — Clean TypeScript throughout

---

## Why Sui

Morita was built specifically for Sui because no other chain provides this combination:

| Primitive | How Morita Uses It |
|-----------|-------------------|
| **Enoki (zkLogin)** | Google OAuth → instant Sui wallet. No extension, no seed phrase. |
| **Enoki (Sponsored Tx)** | Platform pays all gas. Users never touch SUI. |
| **Sui Kiosk** | Royalties enforced at protocol layer. Cannot be bypassed. |
| **Programmable Transaction Blocks** | Atomic cross-game barter in 1 transaction. |
| **Walrus** | Decentralized item metadata. Immutable, permanent, millisecond access. |
| **Object Model** | Every GameItem is a first-class object. Safety guaranteed by Move. |
| **gRPC + GraphQL** | Modern data access. No deprecated JSON-RPC. |

---

About:

Tideline is Sui's first and only American style covered call options protocol.

The protocol lets you SELL covered calls using an RFQ service.

It lets you buy and sell these calls as tokens either through RFQ or through Deepbook.

It also lets you passively invest in an auto-compounding vault.

Why Traditional Options?
1) It unlocks the ability to earn yield by selling covered calls.

2) It enables trading strategies that weren't previously possible.

3) It provides another trading venue with capped downsides.

Everything is live at "https://tideline.finance" was built 100% during this hackathon.

This includes:

- ALL of the protocol's Move contracts.

- ALL of the frontend.

- ALL deployment & CI infra.

- ALL monitoring infra.

- ALL 12 backend micro services

That's correct, we have 12 micro services powering the backend.

We've also implemented an experimental "Session Wallet" that allows user's to bring any non-Sui ed25519 compatible wallet. The wallet signs a message that gets verified on chain, a Account object gets created and tied to the other wallet's public key, a temporary sui wallet gets created and a SessionCap object gets minted back to this temp wallet. The user can interact with the protocol WITHOUT needing to sign transactions or messages. EXCEPT for withdraw.

About:

SuiPayroll is an on-chain payroll protocol built on the Sui blockchain, designed for DAOs and remote crypto teams who need a better way to pay contributors.

The Problem:

Most Web3 teams still pay contributors manually — one wallet transfer at a time. No proof of payment. No audit trail. No structure.

The Solution:

SuiPayroll lets any employer deposit funds into a Move smart contract and pay their entire team simultaneously in one transaction. Every contributor automatically receives a soulbound PayslipNFT — permanent, verifiable, on-chain proof of payment that cannot be transferred or faked.

Key Features:

- One-click payroll — pay your entire team in a single Sui transaction

- SUI & USDC support — pay in native SUI or stablecoin

- Soulbound PayslipNFT — minted to every contributor on payment

- CSV bulk import — upload a spreadsheet and fill the form instantly

- Payroll templates — save your team and run next month in one click

- Gas estimation — dry-run the transaction before depositing

- Real-time address validation — verify wallets are active before sending

Built on Sui for sub-second finality, sub-cent gas fees, and atomic Programmable Transaction Blocks that make multi-wallet payroll possible in a single transaction.

About:

Lastle is a decentralized inheritance protocol built on Sui that helps crypto holders securely pass their digital assets to loved ones.

Users can create an inheritance vault, assign beneficiaries, and define a heartbeat period. As long as the owner remains active, the assets stay under their control. If the owner becomes inactive beyond the configured period, anyone can trigger the protocol to verify the inactivity condition and automatically transfer the assets to the designated beneficiaries.

Unlike traditional inheritance methods that rely on lawyers, custodians, or sharing private keys, Lastle enables trustless and programmable asset inheritance directly on-chain. Assets can remain productive during the owner's lifetime through integrations with DeFi yield protocols, while still being protected by inheritance rules.

By combining self-custody, automation, and transparent smart contracts, Lastle ensures that digital wealth is never permanently lost and can be passed on securely to the next generation.

About:

### Project Description: Protocol Z

**Summary:** Protocol Z is a Web3-powered trade finance escrow engine built on the high-speed Sui Network. It replaces slow, legacy banking rails with automated smart contracts and localized Python data oracles, reducing multi day cross border transaction delays to near instant execution.

### 🌍 The Problem

International trade is a $25 Trillion engine driving the global economy, yet its financial operations remain bogged down by century-old practices:

- **Manual Banking Delays:** Traditional Letters of Credit (LCs) take 10 to 15 days to clear due to manual paperwork verification by intermediary banks.

- **Aggressive Fees:** Intermediary banks and heavy FX spreads extract up to 5% of total shipment values, destroying margins for independent exporters.

- **The Counterparty Trust Gap:** Establishing financial trust between international buyers and sellers requires massive collateral or complex legal structures, leaving smaller enterprise trade setups underserved.

### **The Solution & Technical Architecture**

Protocol Z removes blockchain complexity to deliver a friction-free Web2 user experience backed by Web3 military-grade protection. The full-stack application utilizes a three-tier automated risk management loop to securely automate international trade trust:

- **Seamless Web2 Onboarding via Sui zkLogin:** Exporters and importers onboard instantly using standard Google credentials, removing the barrier of seed-phrase management while preserving corporate privacy via native Zero-Knowledge Proofs (ZKPs).

- **Immutable Code-Enforced Custody:** Trade funds are secured inside a decentralized escrow system managed by **Sui Move Smart Contracts**. This completely eliminates counter party risk the buyer cannot withdraw funds mid-transit, and the seller is guaranteed payment if all contractual obligations are met.

- **The Triple-Risk Verification Oracle (Multi-Data Verification):** Instead of relying on singular tracking numbers, our localized Python automation network runs three distinct verification checks before releasing funds to the exporter:

- **Logistics Verification:** The oracle cross-references live port authority logs and container tracking links to confirm physical arrival.

- **Customs Clearing Validation:** The system parses official digital bills of lading and customs manifests to ensure legal compliance at the destination border.

- **3rd-Party Quality & Inspection Verification:** To protect new importers from receiving defective or fraudulent cargo, the contract remains locked until an independent local testing agency (like SGS or a designated inspector) uploads a cryptographically signed digital inspection report verifying product quality.

**Current Development & Technical Stack**

The repository stands as a fully operational end-to-end local Proof of Concept (PoC) mapped on an Ubuntu Linux environment:

- **Frontend Dashboard:** Next.js 15, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS

- **Backend Bridge:** Node.js API Pipeline (`child_process` command management)

- **Automation Oracle Engine:** Python 3 (`python-dotenv`, CLI parameter mapping)

- **Smart Contract Architecture:** Sui Move Framework

Next Milestones & Grant Resource Allocation

With the support of Sui Foundation grant funding, our immediate 2026 engineering roadmap focuses on:

- **Hardening Infrastructure:** Implementing strict input sanitization on our Node runtime to prevent system injection vulnerabilities.

- **Decentralizing the Oracle:** Transitioning the local Python verification script into a decentralized execution network utilizing cryptographic data signatures before passing variables to the Sui ledger.

- **Smart Contract Auditing:** Moving from a localized test environment to full Sui Testnet deployment backed by professional smart contract audits to guarantee production-grade safety.

**Project Link :**

**GitHub Repository:** `https://github.com/balaji11032006-hub/protocol-Z-Core`

About:

### **The problem**

Tokenizing real-world securities on-chain today forces a bad trade-off:

- **Unregulated wrappers** — fungible tokens that move freely but that no regulator accepts, because anyone can hold and trade them. Not real securities.

- **Compliant, permissioned venues** — assets that enforce eligibility, but sit in isolated allow-list silos with no shared liquidity. Nobody can actually trade them.

You get compliance *or* liquidity, never both.

### **The solution**

LockStream makes a regulated security **compliant and liquid at the same time**, using Sui's two-asset model:

- The security lives as a **closed-loop **`Token<T>` — transferable only between KYC-verified, eligible holders. This is the regulated object of record.

- At the **venue boundary**, a compliance-gated adapter converts the `Token` into a fungible `Coin<T>` — but only after passing on-chain KYC, jurisdiction, and freeze checks. Inside the venue it trades as a plain `Coin<T>` against `Coin<Q>` (e.g. USDC), reaching the **shared DEX liquidity** of DeepBook and Cetus.

- On exit, the adapter **re-wraps** the `Coin` back into the gated `Token`, under the same checks.

The result: the same audited compliance gate, every venue. Compliance is enforced by the contract itself — not a backend that can be bypassed — while the asset reaches real, shared liquidity.

### **How it works (architecture)**

`   Regulated security:  Token<T>   (closed-loop, KYC-only)
                            │  ▲
       deposit: verify ─────┘  └───── exit: re-wrap
       Token → Coin<T>               Coin<T> → Token
       (compliance gate)            (compliance gate)
                            ▼  │
        ┌─────────────────────────────────┐  ← trades inside the venue as a
        │  flows  (compliance envelope)    │     plain Coin<T> vs Coin<Q>,
        │  LegProof · Inflow<T> · Outflow  │     on the shared DEX liquidity
        └─────────────────────────────────┘
            │                │                  │
            ▼                ▼                  ▼
      deepbook_adapter   clmm_adapter        dlmm_adapter
      (DeepBook CLOB)    (Cetus concentrated)(Cetus bins)`
- `flows`** — one compliance envelope, built once.** `LegProof` (per-leg eligibility: requirement + freeze table), `Inflow<T>`(deposit envelope, constructed only through the full verify-to-coin gate), and `Outflow<T>` (withdrawal envelope, constructible only by adapter modules). Mutual exclusivity is the enforcement: a registered (regulated) asset **must** travel the gated path; an open asset cannot even construct the regulated arguments.

- **Thin venue adapters** consume the *same* envelope — so DeepBook's CLOB and Cetus's AMM pools share one audited compliance path. DeepBook serves professional market makers and limit-order traders; the Cetus AMMs let ordinary holders provide passive liquidity too — without forking the compliance logic.

### **What we built**

- **Compliance & identity** — on-chain KYC attestation carrying **jurisdiction + investor class** (no PII ever on-chain); **Reg-S enforced by the contract** (e.g. a US-resident wallet is blocked on every leg); guardian controls to **freeze an account, pause the market, or force a reissue**.

- **Full issuance lifecycle** — issuers create regulated assets, run **IPOs / offerings**, and **pay dividends**; the **cap table (holder roster) lives entirely on Sui**; holders see positions and claim payouts on-chain.

- **Liquidity layer** — the `flows` compliance envelope plus adapters for **DeepBook (CLOB)**, **Cetus CLMM** (concentrated), and **Cetus DLMM** (discrete bins).

- **Web app** — one console per user type: landing, trading, KYC, issuer, investor holdings, and governance.

### **Tech stack**

- **Sui Move** — closed-loop `Token` standard for the regulated security; custom compliance / registry / offering / dividend / rights-ledger modules.

- **DeepBook V3** — on-chain central limit order book (primary settlement venue).

- **Cetus** — CLMM (concentrated liquidity) and DLMM (discrete-bin) AMM pools.

- **Frontend** — Vite + Bun + React + `@mysten/dapp-kit`; wallet-signed transactions; gRPC + GraphQL transport.

### **What's live**

Deployed and demoable on **Sui testnet today** — compliant issuance, on-chain dividends, and a regulated security trading on a real DeepBook order book, with the eligibility checks enforced on-chain.

### **What's next**

- Mainnet deployment and additional regulated asset classes.

- Sui-native multisig for sensitive guardian actions.

- More venues bound through the same compliance envelope.

### **Key features (bullet list, for a features field)**

- Regulated securities as closed-loop, KYC-gated `Token`s on Sui

- On-chain KYC: jurisdiction + investor class, **no PII on-chain**

- **Reg-S enforced by the contract** on every trade leg — not a bypassable backend

- Trades on **DeepBook**'s shared on-chain order book + **Cetus** AMM pools

- One audited compliance envelope, pluggable venues (CLOB + CLMM + DLMM)

- Full lifecycle: issuance, IPOs, dividends, on-chain cap table

- Guardian controls: freeze / pause / reissue

- Live on Sui testnet

About:

Levo is a consumer-grade payments and yield app on the Sui blockchain that hides crypto complexity behind a familiar, USDC-first experience — and extends it with an **agentic layer** that lets users delegate on-chain actions to an autonomous runner agent under cryptographically enforced, on-chain-capped limits.

Sign in with X (Twitter), receive a Privy-backed embedded Sui wallet automatically, and immediately send USDC to any `@handle`, earn yield on idle balances, or grant an agent a narrowly-scoped "mandate" to manage your yield for you. No seed phrases, no browser extension, no gas tokens to think about.

### **1. Motivation**

Stablecoins are the first crypto product with real consumer pull — but the on-ramp to *using* them is still hostile:

- **Wallet friction.** Seed phrases, browser extensions, and gas tokens are the three biggest reasons mainstream users abandon a crypto product before their first transaction. A payments app cannot ask a user to back up 12 words and buy a separate gas token before sending $5.

- **Addresses are not identities.** People know each other by `@handle`, not by `0x9f3c…`. Pasting a 66-character hex string is error-prone and irreversible.

- **Idle balances earn nothing.** Yield in DeFi exists, but it is gated behind vault UIs, LP math, and approval flows that are illegible to a normal user.

- **Automation requires trust you shouldn't have to give.** "Let an agent manage my money" today usually means handing over a private key or an unlimited token approval. That is the opposite of safe.

Levo's thesis is that **the wallet should disappear**. Identity comes from an account the user already has (X), the balance is denominated in dollars (USDC), settlement and gas are handled by the protocol, and *delegation to automation is bounded by the chain itself* rather than by trust in an operator. The result is an app that feels like Venmo with a savings account, but settles on a public ledger and can safely act on the user's behalf.

### **2. What Levo does**

**SurfaceWhat the user doesWhat happens underneathSign in**"Continue with X"Privy provisions a persistent, server-managed embedded Sui wallet keyed to the user. No extension, no seed phrase.**Send**Enter an `@handle` + USDC amountLevo resolves the recipient's canonical Sui wallet from their X identity and settles funds into their **address balance**.**Earn**Tap "Earn" on idle USDCUSDC is deposited into StableLayer; the user sees a single USDC number and a claimable yield figure — no LP tokens, no vault jargon.**Swap**Convert between assetsRouted through the 7K aggregator; output settles back to the user's address balance.**Lookup**Search an `@handle`Public resolution of a recipient's wallet and recent incoming activity, so a sender can verify before paying.**Activity**View historyUnified sent/received feed with on-chain delivery status.**Agent (mandate)**Grant an agent permission to run Earn actionsA **Mandate** object is created on-chain with hard caps (per-transaction, per-period, allowed actions, allowed targets, expiry). The agent can act only within those bounds, enforced by Move + Seal.

The defining UX choice is **USDC-first**: the app surfaces dollars, not coins. Gas, coin-object selection, and settlement primitives are abstracted away so the mental model stays "money in, money out."

### **3. Architecture**

Levo is a pnpm monorepo with three pillars: a Next.js web app (`apps/web`), Sui Move packages (`packages/levo-agent`, `packages/levo-usd`), and deployment/ops tooling (`infra`, `scripts`).

`apps/web            Next.js 16 (App Router) — UI, API routes, agent runtime
packages/levo-agent Move package: mandate engine + Seal witness chain
packages/levo-usd   LevoUSD settlement coin for StableLayer
infra / scripts     Docker, rsync deploy, gas-station + mainnet bootstrap
`
### **3.1 Identity & embedded wallets**

Authentication is **X (Twitter) OAuth via Privy**, which also provisions an embedded Sui wallet per user. Levo deliberately chose Privy over **zkLogin**: zkLogin derives an address from an OAuth nonce and Sui epoch, so an expired JWT or epoch rotation can change the derived address — a recipe for "lost funds" support tickets in a consumer payments product. Privy gives a **stable, server-managed address** that survives session and token churn, and enables server-side signing for gas sponsorship and agent execution without a browser extension. zkLogin remains a viable *optional advanced path* later.

### **3.2 Settlement: native Sui Address Balances**

All user-facing value movement settles through **Sui Address Balances** via `0x2::coin::send_funds<T>`. Recipients receive a canonical *per-address balance* rather than an app-managed `Coin` object. This matters because:

- recipients get a single, mergeable balance instead of a pile of dust coin objects to manage;

- delivery is verifiable from `balanceChanges` (with an accumulator-merge-event fallback) without requiring `objectChanges`;

- it removes a whole class of coin-fragmentation and ownership edge cases.

Protocol inputs that *must* be `Coin` objects (e.g. StableLayer deposits, 7K swap inputs) still use SDK coin selection — Address Balances are used for the **outputs/settlement** legs.

### **3.3 Gas sponsorship**

Users never hold SUI. A **gas station** sponsor signer pays for transactions, preferring **address-balance gas** when the network feature flag is enabled and the sponsor's SUI address balance is funded, with legacy `Coin<SUI>` gas as an explicit fallback. `/api/health` and `gas-station:status` report signer validity, address-balance gas readiness, and fallback availability.

### **3.4 The agentic layer: mandates + Seal witness chain**

This is what makes Levo more than a wallet. A user can delegate actions (today: Earn deposit / withdraw / harvest) to an autonomous **runner agent** — but the delegation is bounded *by the chain*, not by trust.

**Mandate object (**`mandate.move`**).** An on-chain capability the owner grants to a specific agent address, carrying:

- an **actions bitfield** (which action types are permitted; V1 gates everything except `earn_*`);

- **coin limits** per coin type — `perTxCap` and a rolling `periodCap` with `periodSpent` / `periodStartMs` accounting;

- **allowed targets** (non-empty by construction — an empty allowlist is rejected to avoid a "permit anything" footgun);

- an **expiry** and a **revoked** flag (owner can revoke or update expiry at any time);

- a **witness commit** and monotonic **nonce** anchoring a hash chain.

**Seal witness chain.** Every privileged action must pass through a one-time, replay-resistant approval:

- The agent requests authorization from **Seal** (Mysten's threshold-MPC decryption committee). `seal_policy::seal_approve<C>` is a read-only predicate that re-derives the requested identity and asserts the action, target, coin type, amount, and *next commit* all bind to the live mandate state — so an old Seal blob can't be replayed against a different action.

- Consuming the witness (`payment::consume_witness<C>`) returns a `WitnessReceipt` — a *hot potato* with no abilities, which forces the action to be authorized and executed **atomically inside a single PTB**.

- Execution **rotates the witness commit** to the next link in the hash chain and bumps the nonce, so each authorization is single-use. Commits are action-bound: a witness preimage is cryptographically tied to `(mandate_id, action, target, coin_type, amount, next_commit)`.

The package ships with a recorded **security audit** (39/39 Move tests passing); the audit drove the action-binding of commits, single-bit action enforcement, limit upper-bounds against overflow self-lock, and the V1 gating to Earn-only actions. Remaining hardening items (UpgradeCap → multisig, owner-co-signed initial witness) are tracked openly.

### **3.5 Off-chain runtime**

The web app hosts the agent runtime: a mandate draft/spec layer, an executor that builds sponsored PTBs, an on-chain explorer, and a **scheduler** that ticks hosted user-agents on a cron loop. State lives in Postgres (Prisma) with Redis for rate limiting and distributed locks.

### **3.6 Tech stack**

- **Frontend:** React 19, Next.js 16 (App Router), Tailwind CSS 4, shadcn/ui

- **Auth & wallet:** Privy (X OAuth + embedded Sui wallets)

- **Chain:** Sui; `@mysten/sui`, Seal, StableLayer SDK, 7K aggregator SDK

- **Contracts:** Sui Move (2024.beta) — `levo_agent`, `levo_usd`

- **Backend:** Next.js API routes, Prisma (PostgreSQL), Redis

- **Testing:** Vitest (web), Move unit tests (contracts), Playwright (e2e)

### **4. Impact**

- **Onboarding collapses to one tap.** "Sign in with X → wallet exists → send dollars" removes seed phrases, extensions, and gas purchases — the three classic drop-off points — from the critical path.

- **Payments addressed by identity.** Sending to `@handle` instead of a hex address makes a public-ledger payment feel like a social transfer, while Address-Balance settlement gives recipients a clean, canonical balance.

- **Idle dollars become productive** without exposing the user to DeFi mechanics; yield is a single number they can claim.

- **Safe automation is the differentiator.** Most "AI agent wallets" require trusting an operator with a key or an unlimited approval. Levo's mandates make the *chain* the enforcement boundary: caps, targets, action types, and expiry are checked in Move, and Seal + a hot-potato witness chain make each authorization single-use and non-replayable. A compromised agent can do no more than a compromised mandate already allows — and the owner can revoke instantly.

- **Auditable by construction.** Witness consumption emits nonce + before/after commit events, letting indexers detect gaps without scanning the full chain.

In short, Levo packages a public-ledger stablecoin wallet as a mainstream payments-and-savings app, and then does the hard part — making delegation to an autonomous agent something a normal user can grant *safely*, because the limits are enforced by the blockchain instead of by trust.

### 116. xterm.fi - The Cross-Chain Liquidity Aggregator on Sui

Media:
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/ee0f23db-3730-48d4-9b22-80d178df08a6/fed4e9c9-828b-44df-ac36-d4d7d5c2d4b9.png

About:

[*xterm.fi*](http://xterm.fi) is a consumer-grade, multi-chain DeFi app — one interface to **swap, bridge, and track capital** across every major chain. Sign in with email or Google, and a full set of wallets — one per chain family (Ethereum & EVM L2s, Solana, Sui, Bitcoin) — is provisioned for you automatically. No seed phrases, no browser extension, no switching networks. Pick two tokens, and [xterm.fi](http://xterm.fi) finds the best route across the entire on-chain market and executes it.

The defining idea: where most of DeFi makes you choose a chain, install a wallet for each ecosystem, and bridge by hand, [xterm.fi](http://xterm.fi) collapses the whole multi-chain world into a single account and a single action — *"find the best route."*

### **1. Motivation**

DeFi has real pull, but the multi-chain experience is hostile to anyone who isn't already an expert:

**Chain fragmentation.** Liquidity, yield, and assets are scattered across dozens of chains and virtual machines (EVM, SVM, Move, UTXO). Users have to know which chain holds what, switch networks constantly, and keep a separate wallet for each ecosystem.

**Bridging is the scariest step.** Moving value across chains means picking a bridge, trusting it, and hoping the route is the cheapest and fastest. It's error-prone, slow to reason about, and the failure modes are intimidating.

**Wallet sprawl.** A serious multi-chain user juggles an EVM wallet, a Solana wallet, a Sui wallet, and a Bitcoin wallet — each with its own seed phrase and its own backup burden.

**Best execution is opaque.** Even on one chain, finding the best price across DEXs is hard. Across chains, doing it by hand is effectively impossible.

**Capital is idle and illegible.** Yield exists everywhere, but it's locked behind a different UI per protocol, and a user's positions are spread across chains with no single place to see — let alone manage — them.

[xterm.fi](http://xterm.fi)'s thesis is that the chain should disappear. Identity comes from an account the user already has (email/Google), wallets are provisioned across every VM, routing is solved by an engine that searches the whole market, and a user's entire multi-chain position lives behind one portfolio. The result feels like *one app for all of crypto* — not a different app per chain.

### **2. What *[xterm.fi](http://xterm.fi)* does**

- **Sign in.** The user taps "Continue with email / Google," and [xterm.fi](http://xterm.fi) provisions a persistent embedded wallet per chain family — Ethereum, Solana, Sui, Bitcoin — keyed to the account. No extension, no seed phrase.

- **Swap.** The user picks two tokens and enters an amount; an aggregation engine searches DEXs on the chain for the best price and executes, with output settling to the user's own address.

- **Bridge.** The user picks a source and a destination chain; the engine finds the optimal cross-chain route across bridges and DEXs, and the destination auto-fills from the user's own wallet on the target chain.

- **Earn.** The user browses or opens an opportunity, and [xterm.fi](http://xterm.fi) surfaces vetted DeFi earn opportunities (vaults, lending, LP) across chains with live APY and analytics, plus on-chain reward discovery — deposit in a few taps.

- **Portfolio.** The user opens the portfolio to see balances and DeFi positions across every chain aggregated into a single consolidated view.

- **Onboard (partners).** The user can enter through a partner flow with white-label onboarding (e.g., Abstract, Soneium, Base) and partner-specific theming.

The defining UX choice is **account-first and chain-agnostic**: you think in tokens and amounts, not in networks and wallets. The destination of a cross-chain move auto-fills from your own account on the target chain, so "send to the right address on the right chain" stops being something you do by hand.

### **3. Architecture**

[xterm.fi](http://xterm.fi) is a pnpm monorepo. The product is a single Next.js app that hosts the UI, a thin API layer, and the multi-chain wallet and signing logic.

### **3.1 Identity & embedded wallets**

Authentication is email/Google via Privy. On login, [xterm.fi](http://xterm.fi) provisions a canonical set of embedded wallets — one per chain family: Ethereum (EVM), Solana, Sui, and Bitcoin (SegWit). The login popup is scoped to identity only; external-wallet clutter is deliberately kept out of the sign-in flow. Each user gets a stable, server-managed address per chain that survives session and token churn — no seed phrases, no extension.

### **3.2 Universal multi-chain coverage (EVM · SVM · Move · UTXO)**

[xterm.fi](http://xterm.fi) spans four virtual-machine families under one account: Ethereum and EVM L2s, Solana (SVM), Sui (Move), and Bitcoin (UTXO). Each account exposes the correct address on every chain, and a cross-chain action auto-fills its destination from that set — so value always lands in an address the user already controls.

### **3.3 Best-route swap & bridge engine**

Every swap and bridge is resolved by an audited cross-chain liquidity aggregation engine that searches DEXs and bridges for the best route by price, speed, and cost. On Sui it routes through native DEXs (e.g., Cetus, 7K/Bluefin); across chains it stitches bridge and swap legs into a single executable route. Route options are filtered by execution guards that drop any venue that can't reliably build a transaction.

### **3.4 Server-assisted signing**

For embedded wallets, signing is brokered securely on the server: the app sends a transaction digest to a signing endpoint that returns a signature without ever exposing a key to the browser. This covers Sui (raw-sign) and Bitcoin (PSBT) flows and lets a user authorize a complex cross-chain route without a browser extension. For users who prefer self-custody on Sui, an independent Sui wallet session runs in parallel and always takes precedence for Sui signing.

### **3.5 Earn & portfolio data**

Earn opportunities and cross-chain positions are aggregated from a DeFi data layer: live APY and analytics for vaults and lending, on-chain reward/campaign discovery, and per-chain position tracking across EVM, SVM, Move, and UTXO — all assembled into a single portfolio. Token prices and balances are resolved through the same engine that powers routing.

### **3.6 Runtime**

The app is a Next.js 16 (App Router) deployment with a thin API layer for proxying market and earn data, brokering embedded-wallet signing, and serving runtime config. State is client-side (Zustand + TanStack Query); there is no application database — wallets are managed by the identity provider and market data comes from upstream APIs. Errors and performance are tracked via Sentry; in-app support via Intercom.

### **3.7 Tech stack**

- **Frontend:** React 19 (React Compiler), Next.js 16 (App Router, Turbopack), Material UI v9 + Emotion, Zustand, TanStack Query/Form, i18next

- **Auth & wallets:** Privy (email/Google + embedded wallets) across EVM, Solana, Sui, Bitcoin; Sui dapp-kit for self-custody Sui

- **Chains & DeFi:** `@mysten/sui`, wagmi/viem, Solana SDK, bitcoinjs-lib; an audited cross-chain liquidity aggregation engine plus a DeFi earn/rewards data layer

- **Backend:** Next.js API routes (proxy + signing), Sentry, pino logging

- **Testing:** Vitest (unit + snapshot), Playwright (e2e), Storybook + Chromatic

### **4. Impact**

**One account for all of crypto.** Email/Google → wallets exist on every major chain → swap, bridge, and earn — without seed phrases, extensions, or per-chain wallet juggling.

**The chain becomes invisible.** Best-route execution and auto-filled destinations turn "which chain, which bridge, which address" into a single action.

**Universal coverage is the differentiator.** Very few apps span EVM, Solana, Sui, and Bitcoin under one identity; [xterm.fi](http://xterm.fi) treats them as one market instead of four.

**Scattered capital becomes legible.** Cross-chain earn plus a consolidated portfolio let a user see and act on everything they hold in one place.

In short, [xterm.fi](http://xterm.fi) packages the entire multi-chain DeFi landscape — swaps, bridges, yield, and portfolio — behind one account and one "find the best route" action, so using crypto stops meaning "pick a chain and a wallet first."

About:

**TRD - The Real Deal**

🌐 [therealdeal.app](https://therealdeal.app/)

🤖 [TRD live Q&A chat assistant](https://notebooklm.google.com/notebook/a8373e01-acbf-4c33-bc84-8abdbe01adcc)

𝕏 [@therealdealhq](https://x.com/therealdealhq)

Target audience: stablecoin-allocator

Pitches for other audiences (micro-asset lender, technologist, tradfi private debt investor)

are available in the [TRD docs](https://therealdeal.app/docs).

**One-liner:**

TRD lets worldwide investors invest in productive assets in East Africa —

starting with electric motorbikes — via the TRD Open-Ended Adaptive Debt

Facility. TRD provides the trust and accounting layer for private credit

— a live Risk Monitor, updated daily from independent

third-party data: the micro-entrepreneurs' real-world productivity from Bolt, and

the lender's aggregated loan book from their Loan Management System.

**One-Paragraph:**

The TRD facility is underwritable because the data is processed inside a Trusted

Execution Environment (TEE) and published to Walrus, so it cannot be quietly

edited — you rely on verifiable performance, not a quarterly PDF the borrower

writes about itself. The Risk Monitor

tracks the facility's covenants and KPIs daily, with a focus on the Coverage Ratio

(the riders' earnings versus debt service) and Portfolio at Risk (the share of the

loan book overdue). A security agent incorporated in the legal system where the assets operate can take

legal action and take over the loan receivables and the collateral — when default occurs.

Our first facility is a collaboration with a Kenyan electric-mobility

lender — the first of many to come.

**One-Page:**

**The problem**

You hold stablecoins and want sustainable, real-economy yield.

Productive-asset lending in East Africa is exactly that opportunity — real assets

creating value in the real economy, real repayment, real demand for capital — but

it has never been investable on your terms. Every path in forces you to trust an originator's quarterly PDF and a stack

of intermediaries you cannot independently verify, each pricing in the uncertainty

they cannot resolve. So good loan books stay underfunded and yield stays locked

behind opacity.

**Why it's new**

A live, daily loan book for a private credit portfolio has

simply never existed — diligence has always meant quarterly, self-reported PDFs.

It is only possible now because the underlying technology just arrived: Sui

Nautilus runs the risk engine inside verifiable TEEs, Seal encrypts sensitive

borrower data with programmable access control, and Walrus stores the attested

output — all anchored on Sui. Make that performance independently verifiable and a

whole asset class opens to worldwide investors who could never touch it before.

**How it works**

The two data streams are triangulated against the NTSA vehicle

registry, so an asset cannot earn on paper while missing in reality. Everything

surfaces on the Risk Monitor — the same live metrics the lender runs internally,

exposed to you with no information asymmetry. The Coverage Ratio flags Safe above

1.2x, At-Risk between 1.0–1.2x, and Undercollateralized below 1.0x, alongside

loan-to-value and per-asset productivity.

**The deal**

What you invest in is the **TRD Open-Ended Adaptive Debt

Facility** — a facility that scales with the lender's verified performance rather

than a fixed, static loan. Our first facility backs a Kenyan electric-mobility

lender financing income-generating motorbikes for Nairobi's

gig-economy riders, with a pipeline scaling across Tanzania, Uganda, and adjacent

productive-asset classes.

**Real-world enforcement**

The corporate structure bridges on-chain and

off-chain. Through a security agent, the facility holds a perfected, in-country,

registrable claim over the loan receivables — an enforceable claim in the

jurisdiction where the assets operate, not a paper promise. So a covenant breach

you see on the Risk Monitor is one someone is legally empowered to act on, not

just flag.

**Why this is different**

It is the order of operations. The risk monitoring is

live before a single dollar is raised, and before the facility is ever opened we

stress-test the scenarios — confirming each one is covered by a playbook, and that

the protection each playbook calls for can actually be enforced. Nothing is

improvised in a crisis. That is how real trust is built: by proof, not marketing

talk and last-minute scrambling. And it is why the facility grows responsibly, at

the pace trust is proven rather than the pace capital is chasing.

**The result**

A tighter trust premium: fewer intermediaries, lower cost of

capital for the originator, and continuously-monitored yield for you — real yield

earned from the assets' productivity in the real economy.

Don't trust our marketing — open the Risk Monitor, inspect the on-chain

loan book and streaming telemetry yourself, and decide whether this is the real

deal.

About:

**Dugong — Crypto in one tweet.**

Dugong turns your X (Twitter) account into a Sui wallet. You send coins, run prediction markets, and pay out rewards just by tweeting — **no app to install, no seed phrase, no gas to worry about.**

**The problem**

Crypto onboarding is still broken: install a wallet, back up 12 seed words, buy coins just to pay gas, paste a long `0x…` address. Billions of social users never make it in.

**How it works**

- Tweet a command, e.g. `@DugongWallet send 10 DUG to @lan`

- The command is verified and signed inside a secure **Nautilus enclave (AWS Nitro, attested on-chain)**

- A **Sui Move** contract only accepts enclave-signed calls — even we can't forge your transactions

- Gas is sponsored by **Enoki**, so users never need to hold SUI

- New recipients? Their wallet is **auto-created** on first receive

**Features (all from a tweet)**

- Send coins — DUG · SUI · WAL · USDC

- Prediction markets — `create market: …`, reply `predict 5 DUG on yes`, winners paid on resolve

- Reward campaigns — reward top repliers, winners `claim`

- Web dashboard — log in with X; balances, full history, everything verifiable on Sui

**Tech:** Sui Move · Nautilus Enclave (TEE) · Enoki sponsored transactions · Rust API + indexer · React dashboard. Live on Sui testnet.

About:

**Decentralized settlement infrastructure for converting stablecoins into local fiat across emerging markets.**

Rails is built for fintechs, consumer apps, DeFi protocols, and cross-border payment systems that need fast, composable settlement — without ever taking custody of user funds.

 **What it does**

Rails turns stablecoins into spendable local currency through an on-chain settlement layer running on Sui.

- **Accepts crypto, pays out fiat.** Today it supports native **SUI, USDC, and USDT**, settling into local currencies including **KES (Kenya), NGN (Nigeria), TZS (Tanzania), and MWK (Malawi)**.

- **EVM-compatible, Sui-settled.** Rails integrates with EVM environments, but the actual settlement — on-chain escrow and decentralized liquidity-provider (LP) payout rails executes on Sui.

- **Runs a trustless settlement flow.** Every payment moves through the same four steps:

    a. An **order is created** on Sui.

    b. **LP capacity is matched** to fund the payout.

    c. **Fiat is paid out** through local payment rails.

    d. **Escrowed value is released** only *after* payout is confirmed.

- **Opens participation to liquidity providers.** Anyone, institutions or individual providers — can become an **LP on Rails Protocol** and supply settlement liquidity into the network.

**Tapp is our reference product built on Rails: it merges the cardholder and merchant experiences into one flow, showing how users pay, merchants accept, and settlement moves through Rails with a Sui wallet and local payout rails underneath.

- Combines two sides of a payment into one product. **Most payment apps pick a lane — either you're a cardholder app or a merchant acceptance app. Tapp puts both the cardholder and the merchant experience into a single flow

**- Cardholders can pay — **spend crypto/stablecoin balances as if they were spending local money.

**- Merchants can accept — **take payment and receive settlement in their local currency.

**- It bridges crypto and everyday spending. **Tapp shows the practical endgame — stablecoins becoming something a person can actually pay with and a merchant can actually accept, in their own currency, in seconds.

**Why it matters**

- **No custody risk.** Funds are never held by Rails. On-chain escrow holds value and releases it only on confirmed payout, so no intermediary can freeze, divert, or lose user money.

- **Trustless by design.** Decentralization is the foundation of durable financial infrastructure. Because orders, matching, escrow, and release all happen on-chain, the system is **transparent, auditable, and verifiable** end to end — not a black box you have to trust.

- **Fast.** Transactions have settled in **seconds**, making crypto-to-fiat practical for real payments rather than slow, batch-style transfers.

- **Composable.** Rails is a settlement *layer*, not a closed app. New payment experiences — like Tapp — can be built on top of the same rails **without touching the core**, so builders ship products instead of rebuilding settlement.

- **Built for emerging markets.** It connects global stablecoin liquidity directly to local currencies and local payout rails, closing the last mile where traditional cross-border infrastructure is slowest and most expensive.
